# Agent coverage gap plan: every task in full (2026-10-07)

The companion to [agents-coverage-gap-plan-2026-10-07.md](agents-coverage-gap-plan-2026-10-07.md). *Revised* lines record corrections made after the critics' review; *Also (final review)* lines are amendments from the final review and take precedence over the text above them where the two differ. Tasks S10-S14 come from the final review's slot definitions, and their *Spec* blocks are those definitions. Phases are written *phase P1* and the corrected structure's modules *corrected M5*, so neither reads as a task id.

### N. Loop mechanics and tools

**Goal.** The learner writes the agent loop by hand on the Gemini SDK, keeps a conversation valid across tool calls (every call answered, thought signatures returned unchanged), keeps tool choice accurate as the tool set grows, makes code the agent's action in a sandbox, and judges API, MCP, code and screen surfaces with numbers.

**Gaps it closes.** LOOP-02 Building the loop by hand, each harness's seams, and choosing a harness; LOOP-06 History integrity: call-response pairing, thought signatures and thread repair; CROSS-02 Tool selection at scale; CROSS-10 Code execution as an action, in a sandbox; CROSS-27 Computer and browser use.

*6 tasks, about 26 author-days.*

#### N1. 10.1 and 10.4: write the agent loop on google-genai, see what the SDK's automatic loop and each framework add on top, and break a thought signature

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P1 (its 10.3 sentence lands in phase P2), PR24 (10.1 'Gemini directly' sub-steps and definitions rows, with STEP=loop and the automatic-calling variant added to I2's gemini_tools.py); PR25 (10.4 Level 0 widget, the middleware cell and the harness rows); PR35 (10.3 sentence) · **After:** I1, I2, A1, A2, B1, B2, A4, A7, D5, F8, H3, K1, K2, K3, L1, N6
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/gemini_tools.py (I2's script) gains STEP=loop: the agent loop written on google-genai in about 40 lines, which the page quotes whole. Settings: gemini-3.6-flash on location global, thinking low, A2's SYSTEM_CALC as the system instruction, and the eight declarations B1's single printer produces (exactly what the LangChain brain sends). Automatic function calling is off. Before each call the loop asks a limits.Meter (allow_model_call); after it, it charges prompt, candidates plus thoughts, and cached tokens, as limits.adk_after_model does. It prints every returned part: the function_call name, args and id (when the API sends one), and the byte length of each part's thought_signature. A reply with no function_call is the answer. Otherwise the loop appends the reply Content exactly as received and runs each call in order through the kit's own code: retrieve through A2's passages path (tools.search with passages=True, tenant acme pinned in the turn context, the turn's ledger), and a calculator through A1's framework-free calc_tools.calc(ctx, name, keys=CHAT_KEYS, **args), so check_args and the notice_end-to-statutory_deadline chain apply. A ToolException becomes {'error': ..., 'status': 'error'}. The loop then appends one user Content of function_response parts carrying each call's name and id. When the Meter refuses a call, the turn ends with limits.STOP_ANSWER and stopped_by, and every call is already answered. Totals: model calls, tokens, rupees (shared/prices.py, USD_INR 85) and rag-api's rupees. No deployed service changes and no switch is added.
  - Same script, STEP=afc: the SDK's own loop. It passes plain Python callables to client.chats.create(...).send_message with types.AutomaticFunctionCallingConfig(maximum_remote_calls=MAX_CALLS, default 3): retrieve bound to acme by a closure over tools.search, plus gratuity_estimate, notice_end and statutory_deadline calling shared/desk_calc.calculate directly. It prints automatic_function_calling_history round by round, the answer and the usage the SDK reports. A second question gives no wage ('I have 7 years 8 months of continuous service. Roughly how much gratuity would I get?') and prints the arguments the SDK actually executed.
  - STEP=loop and STEP=afc take OFFLINE=1 (Rs 0, for CI and page builds). This uses A2's stand-in rag-api fixture from deploy/evals/corpus and a scripted Gemini (google.genai.models.Models._generate_content patched). The scripted Gemini puts a thought_signature on the first function_call part of each reply. Following Google's documented rule, it answers 400 'missing a thought_signature' when a function_call part of the turn in progress lacks one. The offline AFC window prints five facts from google-genai 2.22.0, checked offline on 2026-10-06: (1) a callable's tenant_id parameter is declared to the model (FunctionDeclaration.from_callable_with_api_option); (2) at maximum_remote_calls=2 with a model that always asks for a tool, the returned response still holds function_call parts and no text, and the results of the last executed round never reached the model; (3) a callable that raises becomes {'error': 'Failed to invoke function ... with converted arguments ...'} as data; (4) an unknown function name raises KeyError; (5) usage_metadata covers only the last model call.
  - I2's parts printer, shared by STEP=modes, loop and afc, marks each part's thought_signature. STEP=modes' fifth call (two parallel lookups) then shows the signature on the first function_call part only, as the register asks.
  - deploy/commands/agent_turn.py (B1's script): add a TOOLSET= option passed to brains.build(..., toolset=) (A2's argument), unless A2's PR has already added it, so make agent-turn can run the same eight tools beside STEP=loop.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: the gemini-tools target accepts STEP=loop|afc, Q=, MAX_CALLS= and OFFLINE=1 (guard-project unless OFFLINE=1). It runs in the environment commands/agent-turn.sh derives (RAG_API_URL, RAG_TIMEOUT_S=90, DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA=documind-ui-sa), using one shared helper rather than a copy. Add the row in deploy/mk/README.md.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_gemini_tools.py (I2's file, in the chat-pins step of both CI workflows) gains these cases:
    - the loop appends the reply Content unchanged (thought_signature bytes equal);
    - every function_call gets exactly one function_response with its name and id;

    - the Meter stops the turn before a call over the cap, with no call left open;

  - lessons/10-agents/10.4-adapters/build.py:
    - the Level 0 widget's W dict (build.py:580 today; the widget section starts at :574) gains a 'your loop' column and an 'SDK loop' column, computed from the two OFFLINE runs;

    - a new Level 5 table is built from langchain.agents.middleware.__all__ and google.adk.plugins. Each row is asserted to import from the pinned packages and to point at a kit file that exists.

  - Regenerate deploy/workshop_demos/module_10/lesson_10_1 and lesson_10_4 with tools/build_workshop_demos.py and review their digests. Then run python tools/kit_index.py, so INDEX maps the new steps of gemini_tools.py to 10.1 and 10.4.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1 (corrected: M5 'tool use and tool schema design [10.1]')**, I2's level 'Gemini directly: four calling modes, three built-in tools', as a new sub-step 'The loop by hand' after STEP=modes. The level sits after steps 8-9, so the eight calculator declarations are already familiar.: Reads the loop verbatim (a block() excerpt of gemini_tools.py). Then runs make agent-turn BRAIN=langchain TOOLSET=calculators with the same question. *The learner sees:* Each round of their own loop: - the parts Gemini returned, with a signature on each model message's first function_call part; - retrieve's numbered passages; - notice_end, then statutory_deadline, with the dates desk_calc wrote; - the answer, and the Meter's model calls, tokens and rupees. Beside it they see the LangChain brain's turn on the same declarations. A definitions row names the loop observe-think-act, the shape the ReAct paper (reason, act, observe) made standard, so M7's 'observe-think-act and ReAct' slot can point back here.
  - **10.1 (corrected: M5 'tool use and tool schema design [10.1]')**, Same sub-step: a Change-it (B2's primitive): Replaces the one line that appends the reply unchanged with one that rebuilds the model turn from its function_call parts only. Re-runs STEP=loop, then restores the file with git checkout. *The learner sees:* Call 2 is refused with Gemini's 400 naming the missing thought_signature. This is a dated capture; the build shows the same rule offline through the stand-in. 'What just happened' explains: - the signature rides on the first function_call part of each model message and is checked only for the turn in progress; - the SDK keeps it when the reply is appended whole; - LangChain re-attaches it by tool-call id and, when it is missing, sends Google's documented bypass string (chat_models.py's SKIP_THOUGHT_SIGNATURE_VALIDATOR lines, quoted verbatim). That avoids the 400, but Google says the model loses its earlier reasoning. A pointer leads to 11.2, where the learner finds the signature on their own lane thread (N2). A4's cell 3 sends its passages in the user turn rather than as a fabricated retrieve call (A4 Revised (c)).

- **Proof:** Offline: python -m unittest commands/tests/test_gemini_tools.py passes in the chat-pins step of both CI workflows. This includes a scripted two-round STEP=afc run that prints 2 rounds and their arguments from chat.get_history(), fact (6) (the call left unanswered at the cap), and a stand-in s.17(2) passage reaching statutory_deadline. 10.1's build runs STEP=loop and STEP=afc with OFFLINE=1, and runs the Change-it on B2's patched copy. It asserts four things: every function_call is answered once with its id; statutory_deadline's offline result equals desk_calc's date and is not an error; the six AFC facts print; the Change-it run ends in the stand-in's 400, labelled as the stand-in's rule. 10.4's build asserts three things: the widget cells come from those runs; the Level 5 table has a row for every Middleware or Emulator class in langchain 1.4.0's __all__ and for every listed ADK plugin; each row's kit file exists or the row reads 'after <task>'. On the author's lane, as a dated capture: STEP=loop answers A7's chained question, and the first function_call part of every model message in the run carries a non-empty thought_signature. If any does not, the build's lane check fails and the page withdraws its signature claims. The Change-it prints a 400 whose text names thought_signature. If the API accepts the unsigned turn, that claim is withdrawn and the page says what the lane did. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.1, 10.3 and 10.4.
- **Risks:** - 10.1 is the page most at risk of passing 90 minutes, because A4, B3, G5, F11 and I2 already add to it. 10.4 is next: A7, E3, B6, G6, I3, D5, K14 and I4 already land there, and I3 cites 9,318 visible words. Both pages go into the timed rehearsal. The AFC sub-step prints as one window, the 10.4 table is collapsed, and the decision names where each part moves if needed. - The 400's wording, and whether thinking low returns signatures at all, can change with the model or differ between Vertex's global endpoint and the Gemini API. The page asserts only offline structure and shows the lane's dated capture. - The offline AFC facts patch google-genai's private Models._generate_content. The pins hold them, and the tests fail first on an upgrade. - LangChain sets its bypass string as a str after construction, while ADK's constant is bytes. The rehearsal records which form the API accepts before the page says anything about ADK's. - The hand loop runs one message's calls in order, where ToolNode runs them at once. The page says so and points to K7's cell A. - A learner run costs a few model calls plus one or two rag-api passage reads. The page prices it from the run's own Meter and never estimates.
- **Decision:** (1) Placement. Recommended: put the loop and the SDK's loop in 10.1's 'Gemini directly' level, where the learner also meets create_agent's loop. If 10.1's timed rehearsal passes 90 minutes, I2's level and this task move together to the corrected M5 slot 'parallel tool calls', whose content (I2's fifth call, K7's cell A) is raw-API too. (2) The register proposed showing the 400 on A4 cell 3's fabricated retrieve call. Recommended instead: break the signature on the learner's own loop. A fabricated call is exactly what Google's bypass string is for, while the failure seen in production is a real signature lost when history is rebuilt or stored. A4 cell 3 then takes its Revised (c) fallback. (3) PR placement. The kit change and 10.1 go with PR24 if ready, otherwise in a 10.1 follow-up after PR24. The 10.4 parts go with PR25 or PR36 (10.4-b).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) STEP=afc reads its rounds from chat.get_history() after send_message (each model Content's function_call parts, then the user Content's function_response parts). google-genai 2.22.0's Chat.send_message runs its own AFC loop (chats.py:311-377) and leaves response.automatic_function_calling_history None (checked offline 2026-10-06). A labelled second variant calls client.models.generate_content, which fills that field and logs the SDK's 'not recommended' warning; the page shows the two agree. (b) MAX_CALLS defaults to limits.MAX_MODEL_CALLS (CHAT_MAX_MODEL_CALLS, 12; the SDK's own default is 10), so A7's chained question (retrieve, notice_end, statutory_deadline, then the answer) completes live. The cap stays offline fact (2) at maximum_remote_calls=2. (c) Offline fact (6): when chat AFC stops at maximum_remote_calls, the history ends on the model's last function_call with no response. The next send_message therefore sends user, model(call), user(response), model(call), user(text) (checked offline with Models._generate_content patched). test_gemini_tools.py asserts this, and the page points to K4's repair_thread: the kit repairs the pairing invariant that the SDK's own loop breaks. (d) The offline stand-in rag-api adds Code on Wages s.17(2) (evals/corpus/acme/code_on_wages_2019.md:459-464) to A2's NP-03, LV-07, LV-01 and s.53, so statutory_deadline's 'needs' entry (desk_calc.py:397) is met. The build asserts that the offline statutory_deadline result equals desk_calc.calculate('statutory_deadline', ...) and is not an error, and the 10.4 widget reads that run. (e) test_gemini_tools.py adds three cases: a scripted two-round STEP=afc run prints exactly 2 rounds and their arguments; fact (6); the s.17(2) passage reaches statutory_deadline. (f) The 10.4 Level 5 table has one row for each class in langchain 1.4.0's langchain.agents.middleware.__all__ whose name ends in Middleware or Emulator, excluding the base class AgentMiddleware. There are 16 (__all__ holds 17 such names, counting AgentMiddleware): ContextEditing, FilesystemFileSearch, HumanInTheLoop, LLMToolEmulator, LLMToolSelector, ModelCallLimit, ModelFallback, ModelRetry, PII, ProviderToolSearch, ShellTool, Summarization, TodoList, ToolCallLimit, ToolError and ToolRetry. It adds the four plugins in google.adk.plugins.__all__ and integrations/model_armor's ModelArmorPlugin. The build fails if __all__ gains a class with no row. Each row names the kit's counterpart, or 'none' with the reason. Examples: ToolErrorMiddleware against tools.py's one error contract, PIIMiddleware against desk_rules.mask (10.5), LLMToolEmulator against the scripted-model tests, ShellToolMiddleware against N4's sandbox. When a row's kit code lands with another task (D5, F8, H3, K1, K2, K3 or N3), the row is asserted against its file only once that file exists, and until then prints 'after <task>'. The TodoList and FilesystemFileSearch rows have no kit file and are exempt. (g) The SKIP_THOUGHT_SIGNATURE_VALIDATOR lines (langchain-google-genai 4.4.0 chat_models.py:1125-1128, and the active-loop injection at :1601-1650) are quoted through N6's libblock with a version and line-text assert, never through block(). (after review: Chat.send_message never fills automatic_function_calling_history; MAX_CALLS 3 cuts A7's chain; the SDK leaves an unanswered call at its cap; the stand-in lacks s.17(2); the table was a curated subset; block() cannot read the installed package.)

#### N2. 11.2: where the thought signature lives on your own lane thread, and why losing it is silent in LangChain and loud in ADK

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P3, PR43 · **After:** K8, H2, H9, N1
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/thread_read.py (K8's script): --export writes langchain_core.messages.messages_to_dict(messages). Content blocks and additional_kwargs then travel with the export, and K8's replay reads them back with messages_from_dict. additional_kwargs is where langchain-google-genai 4.4.0 keeps '__gemini_function_call_thought_signatures__', keyed by tool-call id (chat_models.py:159-161, :1984-1993).
  - Same script, a --request flag (make thread ... REQUEST=1, also on an exported FILE=). It prints what langchain-google-genai would send for those messages, using its _parse_chat_history(messages, model=CHAT_MODEL):
    - each Content's role and parts;
    - for each function_call part, 'signature: original (N bytes)', 'signature: sentinel' or 'none';
    - for each AI message, its content form (a string, or k blocks with their types) and its tool-call ids.

    No model call is made; it costs Rs 0.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_thread_read.py (K8's, or new) checks an AIMessage with block content, two parallel tool calls, and a signature map on the first call. It survives JsonPlusSerializer dumps_typed/loads_typed (the checkpoint path) and messages_to_dict/messages_from_dict (the export) unchanged. --request reports 'original' on the first call part and 'none' on the second. With the map removed, it reports the sentinel string read from the pinned module's SKIP_THOUGHT_SIGNATURE_VALIDATOR. All three facts were checked offline on the pinned packages on 2026-10-06.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py, in H2's DatabaseSessionService test on SQLite: add one assertion that an event's function_call part keeps its thought_signature bytes through the store's write and read. ADK v1 stores event.model_dump(mode='json') and restores it with Event.model_validate; the bytes come back equal on the pins (checked offline on 2026-10-06). The assertion guards against an upgrade like the one behind the reported DatabaseSessionService 400.
  - deploy/mk/memory.mk (K8's make thread): pass EXPORT=, FILE= and REQUEST=1 through. Then run python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.2 (corrected: M6 [11.2] 'durable storage', or corrected M6 'checkpoint and resume' if K8's replay level moves there)**, K8's Level 4 step 6 'Read it back at any step, then replay and fork it', after the read-back and before the local replay. Also one row in the Level 1 definitions table.: Runs make thread SESSION=lesson102 AT=<the step before the tool call> EXPORT=~/thread102.json REQUEST=1. Deletes the signature map from the file with one python line. Then runs make thread FILE=~/thread102.json REQUEST=1 again. *The learner sees:* Their own lane thread's tool-calling AI message: its content as blocks, its tool-call ids, the signature map, and 'signature: original (N bytes)' on the first function_call part only. After the edit, 'signature: sentinel' appears where the original was: the string LangChain sends so the request still passes. 'What just happened' explains: - the replay in the next cell is faithful because the signature travelled with the export; - LangChain hides a lost signature: no error, only weaker reasoning; - ADK passes whatever bytes its session holds and substitutes nothing (its bypass constant appears only in its reflect-and-retry plugin), so a store that lost them would fail with the 400 the learner produced in 10.1. A new definitions row covers content blocks and the signature map.
- **Proof:** test_thread_read.py and the extended H2 test pass in the chat-pins CI step. This includes the two-turn case ('sentinel' on the current turn's call part, 'none' on the earlier turn's) and a --request built with use_vertexai=True. 11.2's build runs --request on its stand-in thread, which holds a signed tool call. It asserts 'original' on the first call part, 'none' on the second, and the sentinel after the map is removed. On the author's lane, a REQUEST=1 run on a real lesson102 thread prints 'signature: original' (dated capture). If the lane thread carries no signature, the build's lane check fails and the page states what the lane shows instead. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 11.2.
- **Risks:** - _parse_chat_history is private in langchain-google-genai 4.4.0. It is pinned, and the test fails first on an upgrade. - Pinned google-adk 2.8.0 substitutes no sentinel in its normal flow (only plugins/_reflect_retry_model_plugin.py uses one) and passes on whatever bytes its session holds. Gemini checks signatures only for the turn in progress, and ADK builds each request of an invocation from memory. So a lossy store bites only a turn resumed in a later request, such as a tool confirmation or a long-running tool. The page states that condition, not a general loud failure. - K8's blob decoding depends on langgraph-checkpoint-postgres 3.1.2. - H9 re-cuts 11.2, and K8 and B10 add steps to it. This task adds one cell to K8's step and lands in PR43's sequence.
- **Decision:** (1) Whether the 'delete the map' sub-step runs on the page. Recommended: yes; it costs Rs 0 and one line. (2) Whether to keep the one ADK assertion in H2's test. Recommended: keep it. It is one line in a test H2 already writes, and it guards the resumed-turn case (a tool confirmation or a long-running tool), where a store that lost the bytes would surface as Gemini's 400.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) --request calls _parse_chat_history(messages, model=CHAT_MODEL, use_vertexai=True), matching the vertexai=True that shared/profile.build_llm passes. (b) It marks each AI message as inside or outside the active loop: the model messages after the last user message with text (chat_models.py:1601-1650). The sentinel is injected only inside the active loop, so an earlier turn's call part whose map was removed reports 'none', not 'sentinel'. (c) test_thread_read.py adds a two-turn export: with the map removed, the current turn's call part reports 'sentinel' and the earlier turn's reports 'none'. (d) 11.2's stand-in thread (K8's stand-in database) gains a tool-calling AIMessage with block content and a signature map, so the build's --request check reads a signed call. (after review: The sentinel goes only into the active loop, the kit's client uses vertexai=True, and the stand-in thread must hold a signed call for the build check to mean anything.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend 11.2's step: after deleting the map, the learner sees 'sentinel' on the current turn's call part and 'none' on earlier turns' call parts, because LangChain resends the sentinel only inside the active loop. 'What just happened' and the definitions row say two things. Gemini validates signatures only for the turn in progress. ADK builds every request of an invocation from its in-memory session. So a session store that lost the bytes matters only when a turn resumes in a later request, such as a tool confirmation (D5's adk_request_confirmation) or a long-running tool; that matches the reported DatabaseSessionService 400. The page no longer says ADK fails loudly in general. (after review: 'ADK would fail loudly' overstated it: signatures are checked only for the current turn, and ADK builds an invocation's requests from memory.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Adds the earlier-turn case and the use_vertexai argument, and requires a signed call in the stand-in thread.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to M after review (An export change, --request, a new test file, the H2 assertion, mk pass-through, a stand-in thread with a signed call, an 11.2 sub-step and a lane capture are more than one day.)
- **Revised:** risks revised after review (The earlier risk asserted that ADK fails loudly, which holds only for a resumed turn.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (Option (2)'s rationale relied on the overstated 'fails loudly' claim.)
- **Closes:** LOOP-06

#### N3. 10.1: tool selection at scale, measured: every tool, a cheap selector, or the router's toolset

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P1, PR24 · **After:** A4, I2, B1, A1, N1
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/gemini_tools.py (I2's script) gains STEP=scale. It absorbs A4's cell 3b, so the page cell becomes one make line. Each run is one google-genai call on gemini-3.6-flash, location global, with jn-03's question and its LV-07 and NP-03 passages in the user turn (A4's Revised (c) form). Four variants:

    (i) coarse: one calculate(name: enum of the seven, args: object) declaration (A4's);

    (ii) all: the eight declarations B1's printer produces, plus A4's 40 decoys from a fixed seed. The decoys are named as if from two other MCP servers (payroll_*, travel_*), and three are near-synonyms of encashable_days;

    (iii) select: one gemini-3.1-flash-lite call whose response_schema is a list of at most MAX_TOOLS (default 5) of the 48 names as an enum, with retrieve always kept; then the main call carries only those declarations;

    (iv) routed: the declarations of desk_agent.TOOLSETS['handbook'] (desk_agent.py:279-281), read at run time. This is the selection a router has already made for free.
  - For each variant the script makes REPEAT runs (default 5) and prints: declarations per main call and their tokens (count_tokens); the selector's own tokens and rupees for (iii); k of REPEAT calls naming encashable_days; k of REPEAT whose arguments desk_calc.check_args accepts against the same LV-07 passage; and rupees per variant (shared/prices.py, USD_INR 85).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_gemini_tools.py gains these cases:
    - the selector request's schema is enum-only over exactly the 48 names, with maxItems MAX_TOOLS;
    - retrieve is kept even when the selector omits it;
    - the main call carries only the selected declarations;
    - the decoys are identical for one seed;
    - langchain 1.4.0's LLMToolSelectorMiddleware(model=<scripted selector>, max_tools=2, always_include=['retrieve']), run inside create_agent, gives the main model only the selected tools plus retrieve. This is the packaged form of variant (iii).
  - lessons/10-agents/10.1-agent-loop/build.py: reads the provider table from langchain's provider_tool_search.py at build time and asserts it has no Gemini entry (today it lists anthropic and openai). Also asserts that LLMToolSelectorMiddleware takes model, max_tools and always_include. Then run tools/kit_index.py and regenerate the 10.1 demos.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1 (corrected: M5 'tool use and tool schema design [10.1]')**, Level 2 step 4 'The contract', in A4's cell 3b 'Granularity and selection at scale', after the Change-it: Runs make gemini-tools STEP=scale REPEAT=5. *The learner sees:* One table with four rows. For each variant: how many declarations the main call carried and their tokens, the selector's own cost, how often the model named encashable_days, and how often check_args accepted its arguments. The table shows the cost of declaring 48 tools and how often near-synonyms win. Whatever their lane shows is printed as theirs; the page's run is a labelled sample, and only declaration counts are asserted at build.
  - **10.1 (corrected: M5 'tool use and tool schema design [10.1]')**, Level 5: the definitions table and the design rules: Reads one definitions row and two rules. *The learner sees:* The definitions row covers: - tool search and deferred loading: the model sees a short index and loads a schema only when it needs one; - LangChain's ProviderToolSearchMiddleware supports this only for Anthropic and OpenAI (its provider table, read at build). On Gemini the options are a selector call, whose packaged form is LLMToolSelectorMiddleware (variant iii), or a router (the Desk's TOOLSETS, which 10.7's arms measure on outcomes); - filters: default deny per caller (12.1, J3) and McpToolset's tool_filter (12.3, F9); - allowed_function_names constrains the choice without shrinking the declarations (I2). Once N4 lands, a pointer adds the managed-platform level's measurement of code that keeps bulky results out of the context. The two rules: bind only what a step needs, and re-measure selection after every change to the tool list or a description.
- **Proof:** test_gemini_tools.py passes with the selector and middleware cases. The middleware case uses a scripted BaseChatModel selector with with_structured_output and asserts that the main model is bound to exactly the selected tools plus retrieve. 10.1's build asserts three things: the provider table has no Gemini entry; LLMToolSelectorMiddleware takes model, max_tools and always_include; the variant table's declaration counts are 1, 48, at most MAX_TOOLS plus retrieve, and the handbook toolset's count read from desk_agent.TOOLSETS. On the author's lane, the check a run can fail: the selector's reply parsed in every one of REPEAT runs, and its set contained encashable_days in at least k of REPEAT runs, with k set after the dry run and recorded in the PR. A failing run prints FAIL and is not captured. The per-variant selection rates appear as a labelled, dated sample, never as a ranking. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.1.
- **Risks:**
  - Selection rates vary by run and by model version, so only declaration counts are asserted and k of 5 is a dated sample. Five repeats cannot separate close variants, and the page says so.
  - The decoys are synthetic. A real host's list, such as Gemini CLI on DocuMind's MCP server (J5), is longer and messier, and the page names that.
  - A learner run is about twenty small calls plus five selector calls. The page states the run's own cost line rather than an estimate.
  - The selector adds latency and a failure point (a malformed selection). The page names this beside LLMToolSelectorMiddleware's on_parsing_failure option.
- **Decision:** (1) Recommended: move A4's cell 3b into gemini_tools.py, giving one tested implementation, a one-line page cell and no long heredoc. The alternative is to keep the heredoc and add variant (iii) to it, as the register proposes. (2) Recommended: keep the routed variant (iv). It measures the Desk's TOOLSETS on selection accuracy, which A9's outcome arms do not. (3) The selector model and MAX_TOOLS. Recommended: gemini-3.1-flash-lite and 5.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) The LLMToolSelectorMiddleware test's scripted selector is a BaseChatModel subclass whose with_structured_output(schema) returns a runnable that yields a fixed selection. The middleware calls selection_request.model.with_structured_output(schema) (tool_selection.py:409), which the stock fake chat models do not implement. The test asserts that the main model is bound to exactly the selected tools plus retrieve. (b) This amends A4. A4's 'Also (final review, for BENCH-T03)' cell 3b becomes the one line make gemini-tools STEP=scale, owned and tested here. A4 keeps its two Level 5 design rules and builds no heredoc or decoys of its own. (c) For variant (iii), STEP=scale prints whether each selector reply parsed and which set it chose. (after review: The stock fake models cannot act as the selector, and without an amendment on A4 both tasks would build cell 3b.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: Level 5's definitions row says that the provider table read at build has no Gemini entry (langchain 1.4.0's provider_tool_search.py lists anthropic and openai). It does not claim to show why. For variant (iii), the STEP=scale table adds how many selections parsed and how many contained encashable_days. (after review: The pinned source shows only that Gemini has no entry, not why; the new gate needs its counts on the page.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The old lane gate could not fail, because the enum schema forbids outside names and fewer declarations always cost fewer tokens.)
- **Closes:** CROSS-02

#### N4. Managed platform: code as the agent's action, in a sandbox whose isolation you probe, with regulated figures still computed by checked code

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P4, PR65 (I7's 'The same peer on Agent Engine' level, or the managed-platform lesson if G-decision 2 splits it out; plus one pointer sentence in 10.1) · **After:** I1, I5, I7, I2, R7
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_engine.py (I5's and I7's script), sandbox-run: the two-line computation becomes a set of isolation probes. They run with no model, through client.agent_engines.sandboxes.execute_code, on a sandbox created with a short ttl in the existing instance. The probes:
    - a computation;
    - DNS for a public name, a TCP connect to 169.254.169.254:80, and an HTTP GET to metadata.google.internal with Metadata-Flavor: Google, each of which must fail;
    - no environment name matching GOOGLE|AWS|TOKEN|KEY|SECRET|CRED, and no ~/.config/gcloud;
    - a file written in call 1 is read back in call 2, and is absent in a second, fresh sandbox;
    - a file the code writes comes back as an output file, and nothing else does;
    - PROBES=limits adds a sleep past, and an allocation over, the limits I1 records.

    Each probe prints PASS or FAIL against the expected isolation, and both sandboxes are deleted in a finally block. The step stays rehearsal-gated, like I7's level, but is no longer optional inside it.
  - deploy/commands/code_action.py (new), STEP=exits. It generates a seeded synthetic exits file of 1,000 rows (employee id, monthly wage, years, months, fixed_term, term_expired; no names) and computes the reference with shared/desk_calc.gratuity_estimate: the total and the count not eligible. It builds an ADK LlmAgent on gemini-3.6-flash (location global) with code_executor=AgentEngineSandboxCodeExecutor(sandbox_resource_name=<a sandbox it creates>, optimize_data_file=True), and attaches the file as text/csv. ADK 2.8.0 then replaces the file in the request with an 'Available file' placeholder and hands it to the sandbox (flows/llm_flows/_code_execution.py). Two runs:
    - RUN=own: the model writes its own formula;
    - RUN=calc: desk_calc.py (stdlib only) is staged as an input file through session state (CodeExecutorContext's input files), and the instruction says to call desk_calc.calculate for every figure.

    The script prints each code block and its stdout, the agent's total and not-eligible count against the reference, the first rows that differ, and model calls, tokens and rupees from an after-model callback that counts as limits.adk_after_model does. It also prints the request's prompt tokens beside count_tokens of the same question with the file inline. It always passes a sandbox_resource_name, so ADK never auto-creates an Agent Engine or a one-year sandbox, and it deletes the sandbox in a finally block.
  - Same script, STEP=probe-builtin: sends the same probe code through Gemini's built-in code_execution with a 'run exactly this code' prompt. A probe whose executable_code differs from the code sent is marked 'not run verbatim'.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk (I7's targets): sandbox-run gains PROBES=, and a new code-action target takes STEP=exits|probe-builtin and RUN=own|calc. Add it to .PHONY and to the mk/README.md row. No executor is added to any deployed agent, and built-in tools stay demo-only (decision 26).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_code_action.py (new, in the chat-pins CI step, needs no aiplatform). It uses a fake BaseCodeExecutor and a scripted ADK model that emits a python block, and asserts:
    - the PASS/FAIL logic (a fake 'reachable' output is a FAIL);
    - the seeded file and the reference are deterministic;
    - the per-row comparison;
    - a sandbox_resource_name is always passed, and the delete runs after an error;
    - no CSV row reaches any request the scripted model receives.
  - lessons/12-protocols/12.3-a2a-peer/build.py: a table built from the pinned sources at build time.

    Rows from ADK 2.8.0:
    - BuiltInCodeExecutor;
    - AgentEngineSandboxCodeExecutor;
    - GkeCodeExecutor (gVisor job or Agent Sandbox mode; cpu_limit 500m, mem_limit 512Mi, timeout_seconds 300);
    - CloudRunSandboxCodeExecutor (integrations/cloud_run; runs only inside a Cloud Run container; allow_egress False);
    - ContainerCodeExecutor (network_enabled False, capabilities dropped, timeout_seconds 300);
    - VertexAiCodeExecutor;
    - UnsafeLocalCodeExecutor.

    From langchain 1.4.0: ShellToolMiddleware's execution policies (DockerExecutionPolicy has networking disabled by default).

    Columns come from field defaults and docstrings: network, limits, data files and state. Every row is labelled 'documented, not run' except the two the level runs.
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (I1b, authoring repo only) adds rows for: the sandbox's pandas import, which optimize_data_file's preprocessing needs; whether an input file can be imported as a module; which written files come back as outputs; and the documented time and memory limits. Then run python tools/kit_index.py and regenerate the 12.3 demos.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3 (corrected: M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, I7's level 'The same peer on Agent Engine', a new sub-step 'Code as an action, in the platform's sandbox', after the sessions and Memory Bank cells and before make agent-engine-peer-down: Runs make sandbox-run, then make code-action STEP=exits RUN=own, then RUN=calc. *The learner sees:* A PASS line per isolation property on their own lane: no egress, no metadata token, no credential, nothing kept across sandboxes, and only the files the code writes leave. Then one code action doing the work of 1,000 calculator calls: - in RUN=own, the model's formula is compared with desk_calc row by row. Any rows that differ show which rules of s.53 the formula missed: the five-year bar, a part-year over six months, fixed-term pro rata; - in RUN=calc, the code calls desk_calc and its total equals the reference. The model's request carried the 'Available file' placeholder and a short summary instead of the file, shown beside the token count the file would cost inline. Each run shows its model calls, tokens and rupees.
  - **12.3 (corrected: M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, Same sub-step: the executor table and Level 5 lines; plus one pointer sentence in 10.1's 'Gemini directly' level (I2's built-in code execution): Reads the table, and optionally runs make code-action STEP=probe-builtin. *The learner sees:* Seven executors and LangChain's shell policies compared on network, credentials, limits, files and state, with the two run on their lane marked as run. Optionally, the same probes through Gemini's built-in code execution. Level 5 explains: - code as action composes many operations in one step and keeps bulky data out of the context; - a regulated figure still comes from a checked function, so the tenant agent keeps its calculators and built-in tools stay demo-only. 10.1's built-in code-execution paragraph points here.
- **Proof:** test_code_action.py passes in CI. It imports desk_calc from a temporary directory holding only the four staged files, and runs a scripted ADK model with a fake executor on InMemoryRunner. It asserts the CSV's explore_df event in both runs, reads output files from artifact_delta, and checks the reference's three counts on the seeded file. It also asserts that a sandbox_resource_name is always passed, that the delete runs after an error, and that no CSV row reaches any request. On the author's lane, as a dated capture: every isolation probe prints PASS, and a reachable network, a metadata token or a credential prints FAIL and withdraws the level. RUN=calc's total, not-eligible count and conditional count equal desk_calc's reference exactly. The request's prompt tokens are below the inline count. No sandbox remains after the run, as listed through the API. The 12.3 build asserts every executor-table cell against the pinned source read with ast. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 12.3.
- **Risks:**
  - The sandbox's behaviour is documented only in part: no network, a limited file system, 100 MB in and out per request, and state kept up to 14 days. I1b records pandas, input files and output files before the page claims them. If pandas is absent, both runs use optimize_data_file off and read the CSV with the csv module, which loses the token comparison.
  - The model may not follow 'use desk_calc'. RUN=calc's equality check then prints FAIL in the capture, and the page claims nothing.
  - Sandbox time is billed, and its price is unverified until I1 reads it. The cost line comes from the run.
  - ADK's executor auto-creates an Agent Engine and a one-year sandbox when no name is given. The script always names one and deletes it.
  - The synthetic file holds ids only.
  - 12.3 is long. In the corrected structure this level belongs to the managed-platform page.
- **Decision:** (1) Placement. Recommended: a sub-step of I7's 12.3 level now, and the corrected M7 'Google's managed agent platform' page in the corrected structure. The alternative is the corrected M7 'coding agents' page. (2) The data. Recommended: the seeded 1,000-row exits file, which exercises every s.53 rule with desk_calc as the reference. The alternative is the learner's own agent-eval run. (3) STEP=probe-builtin. Recommended: optional on the page; the alternative is the author's capture only. (4) Staging desk_calc.py. Recommended: through ADK's session-state input files, which does not rely on the sandbox keeping files. The alternative is one direct execute_code call, which needs I1's persistence row.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) RUN=calc stages desk_calc.py together with cases.py, desk_law.py and roles.py. desk_calc imports cases, which imports desk_law and roles; all four are stdlib at import time, and cases' firestore and audit imports are lazy. test_code_action.py imports desk_calc from a temporary directory holding only those four files and computes gratuity_estimate(52000, 7, 8) = 240000. Decision (4)'s staging covers all four files. (b) ADK's pre-processor stops at the first unprocessed input file with no loader (flows/llm_flows/_code_execution.py:221-236), and only text/csv has a loader. So the .py files are staged with their names already in CodeExecutorContext.add_processed_file_names, or staged after the CSV. Their contents are base64-encoded the way CodeExecutionUtils.get_encoded_file_content does. Both runs assert the CSV's explore_df event, so the summary and the token comparison are like for like. (c) code_action.py runs on InMemoryRunner (or Runner(artifact_service=InMemoryArtifactService())). Without an artifact service, _post_process_code_execution_result raises 'Artifact service is not initialized.' for any executor other than the built-in one (_code_execution.py:472-473). Output files are read from each event's actions.artifact_delta, and test_code_action.py uses the same construction. (d) The reference is defined in three counts. total is the sum of value where eligible is True. not_eligible counts eligible False. conditional counts eligible None: a fixed term under five years without term_expired. It is printed apart and never summed. The instruction states the same rule. The seeded file holds every case: five years or more, under five, a part-year over six months, a fixed term that expired, and one that did not. (e) The 12.3 executor table reads field defaults and docstrings by parsing the pinned source with ast, never by importing it. ContainerCodeExecutor and GkeCodeExecutor need google-adk[extensions] (docker, kubernetes) and raise ImportError in the chat pins. The table adds a row for ADK 2.8.0's environment toolset (tools/environment/_environment_toolset.py over LocalEnvironment, E2BEnvironment and DaytonaEnvironment), labelled 'documented, not run'. (f) I1b's sandbox row runs agent_engine.py sandbox-run PROBES= rather than its own probe code, so there is one probe implementation. (after review: desk_calc imports cases; ADK's pre-processor would never explore the CSV; the Runner needs an artifact service; the aggregate was undefined; two executors cannot be imported in the chat pins.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The proof now tests the four-file staging, the artifact path and a defined three-count reference.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: I1, I5, I7, I2, R7. R7 is added because 12.3's minutes, and whether this level moves to its own corrected M7 managed-platform page, are decided before it is written. (after review: 12.3 is already heavy, so the page budget must be agreed before this level is written.)
- **Also (final review, CROSS-10):** Kit: PROBES=limits measures the limits instead of testing 'the limits I1 records'. Google's Code Execution overview and quickstart document no CPU, memory or per-execution time limit: only 100 MB of files per request, no network and state for up to 14 days, and execute_code takes only name and input_data. ADK 2.8.0's AgentEngineSandboxCodeExecutor.execute_code passes no timeout (BaseCodeExecutor.timeout_seconds stays None). Inside the sandbox the probe prints os.cpu_count(), /sys/fs/cgroup/cpu.max and memory.max (or 'not exposed'), and the size at which an allocation grown in 256 MiB steps, capped at 2 GiB, fails. Then one execution that sleeps 30 s runs under a 10 s deadline set in agent_engine.py. The call waits in a worker thread, as limits.timed_tool_call waits for a tool, and the finally block deletes the sandbox. Lesson: the sub-step runs make sandbox-run PROBES=limits instead of make sandbox-run. The learner sees three more lines on their own lane, labelled 'measured, not documented': CPU, memory, and 'stopped by your deadline at 10 s'. They sit beside the executor table's source defaults: GkeCodeExecutor cpu_limit 500m, mem_limit 512Mi, timeout_seconds 300; ContainerCodeExecutor timeout_seconds 300. Level 5 adds one rule: where the platform documents no limit, the caller sets one. Proof: test_code_action.py asserts that the deadline ends a fake execute_code that never returns, and that the delete still runs. The dated capture shows the three lines, and an execution that outlives the deadline prints FAIL.
- **Closes:** CROSS-02, CROSS-10

#### N5. Managed platform: API, MCP, code or a screen? Computer and browser use judged with numbers

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **Lands in:** phase P4, PR65 (its last sub-step, plus the computer-use row of I2's three-row table in 10.1) · **After:** I1, I7, N4, D5, F10, I2, R7 (R7 added)
- **Kit:**
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (I1b, authoring repo only, never published) gains a Computer Use row. It records whether a computer-use model is served to the lane, and on which location. It then creates one Agent Platform Computer Use sandbox with an explicit sandbox name and the existing instance, so nothing is auto-provisioned. It uses google-adk 2.8.0's AgentEngineSandboxComputer (integrations/vmaas: a BaseComputer backed by the platform's sandbox browser, 1280x720, with a default ttl of one hour). It runs one ComputerUseToolset episode on the fixed target the author picks, records steps, screenshots, tokens, rupees, seconds and every safety confirmation the model asks for, and deletes the sandbox. A CAPTCHA or a login wall ends the episode and is recorded as the finding; the probe never solves one.
  - plan/agents-section-uplift-register-2026-10-05.md: BENCH-T24 moves from defer to fix with N5, and its 'unverified on Vertex' note is replaced by I1's dated row. The learner kit does not change: the kit's agents never take the GUI path.
  - lessons/12-protocols/12.3-a2a-peer/build.py: the GUI column reads only from the dated capture (L1's pagebuild.data() file) and carries no numbers when the capture is absent. The build asserts this.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3 (corrected: M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, I7's level, a final sub-step 'API, MCP, code or a screen?' after N4's sub-step: Reads a decision table and the author's dated capture of one computer-use episode. *The learner sees:* Four action surfaces side by side: a custom tool call, an MCP call (their own 12.3 task and 12.1 retrieve, with calls and rupees), code as action (their N4 run), and a GUI agent (the captured episode: a screenshot per step, the steps, tokens, rupees and seconds, and the confirmations it asked for). The table's rows cover: - when each surface is right: a GUI agent only when no API or MCP tool exists, such as a legacy HR portal holding a leave balance; - what the model reads: screen text is untrusted input, just as 12.5's door map treats documents; - side effects: every click is an action, and ADK's require_confirmation, which they flipped in 10.4 with D5, is the per-step gate; - isolation: use the platform's sandbox browser, never your own signed-in browser; - provenance: a screen is not a citation. Every DocuMind capability has an API and an MCP tool, so its agents never need the screen.
  - **10.1 (corrected: M5 'tool use and tool schema design [10.1]')**, The computer-use row of I2's three-row table (custom tool, built-in tool, computer use) in the 'Gemini directly' level: Reads the computer-use row. *The learner sees:* One line saying the tenant agent does not use computer use, with a pointer to the judgement and its numbers in M7's managed-platform level, so the topic has one home.
- **Proof:** I1's facts table has a dated Computer Use row: either Confirmed by an episode, or Unverified with the step that failed (no model served, no sandbox in the region, a CAPTCHA or a login wall). The 12.3 build asserts that every number in the GUI column comes from that capture. Without a capture, the column carries documented facts and no numbers, while the API, MCP and code-action columns come from the learner's own runs. The build also asserts the safety rows against the pinned source: the safety_decision branch, allow_private_network_access defaulting to False, and excluded_predefined_functions. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 12.3 and 10.1.
- **Risks:**
  - A computer-use model may not be served on the lane or in the sandbox's region. The level then ships the table with documented facts and no numbers, and says so.
  - A public target page can change, block automation or show a CAPTCHA. The capture is dated, and the probe never solves a CAPTCHA.
  - Screenshots are billed as image tokens at every step. The episode runs once, on the author's lane, and learners do not run it.
  - Google's naming drifts between Agent Engine, Agent Runtime and Agent Platform.
- **Decision:** (1) Recommended: capture one episode on the author's lane, if I1 finds a computer-use model. The alternative is to ship documented facts only. (2) The target. Recommended: the public India Code page for the Code on Wages s.17(2), the same fact the learner retrieved through rag-api and MCP, for a like-for-like comparison. The alternative is a static 'legacy portal' page, if the sandbox can load one without the public internet. (3) Confirm moving BENCH-T24 from defer to fix in the uplift register.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: the probe builds ComputerUseToolset with allow_private_network_access=False (the default, under which navigate refuses private addresses) and with excluded_predefined_functions naming the actions the episode must not take. It records each confirmation raised by the model's safety_decision. ComputerUseTool asks for confirmation only when safety_decision is require_confirmation (computer_use_tool.py:111-125, through tool_context.request_confirmation), which is the same adk_request_confirmation mechanism D5 shows. A before_tool_callback logs every click, to show what gating each click would take. The 12.3 build asserts these three facts against the pinned source. Decision (2)'s alternative target, a private 'legacy portal' page, needs allow_private_network_access=True, a deliberate widening that the page would name. (after review: ComputerUseTool asks for confirmation only on the model's safety_decision, and the toolset's own safety layer was missing.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend 12.3's table: (a) Side effects: the per-step gate is ADK's ToolConfirmation (adk_request_confirmation), triggered by the model's own safety_decision. Gating every click needs a before_tool_callback or a plugin. (b) A new 'safety layer' row: the model's safety_decision, ComputerUseToolset's allow_private_network_access=False default, and excluded_predefined_functions. (c) 'Every capability the agents use has an API' replaces 'every DocuMind capability has an API and an MCP tool'. The MCP server exposes retrieve, list_documents, corpus_stats and calculate_processing_cost, while the Desk, cases and approvals are API-only. The API, MCP and code-action numbers come from the learner's own 12.1, 12.3 and N4 runs. (after review: The page described the gate wrongly, overclaimed MCP coverage, and could not show numbers without a capture.)

#### N6. pagekit: quote a pinned library's lines on a page (libblock), with the version and each line checked, so library excerpts pass check_lesson

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **Lands in:** phase P0, GP02
- **Kit:**
  - pagekit/pagebuild.py gains libblock(dist, rel, start, end=None, n=None, nth=1), block()'s twin for an installed package. It reads rel from importlib.metadata.distribution(dist). It asserts that the installed version equals the kit's pin for dist, parsed from deploy/services/*/requirements*.txt and from the judge venv's install line. It returns a label 'pinned <dist> <version> · <rel>:<first>-<last>' and the lines verbatim. At build time it records {dist, version, rel, first, last, lines, sha256} in the lesson's data/lib_excerpts.json. A build run outside the venv that holds dist fails with a message naming that venv (~/graph-venv or the judge venv).
  - pagekit/check_lesson.py: a code window whose label starts with 'pinned ' is checked against data/lib_excerpts.json, not against deploy/ lines. Every line must be in the excerpt recorded for that label, and the recorded version must equal the kit's pin. Today's verbatim check (check_lesson.py:33-39, 54-73) compares every .py-labelled window with deploy/ lines, so a quoted library window fails. check_lesson stays stdlib and needs no venv.
  - tools/tests/test_libblock.py (stdlib, in checks.yml) covers: the label's shape; a version mismatch refused; the sidecar round trip; check_lesson accepting a pinned window and refusing one whose line is not in the sidecar or whose version differs from the pin.
  - pagekit/README.md documents libblock beside block(). Its users are N1 (langchain-google-genai's sentinel lines), P4 (PlanReActPlanner), P5 (TodoListMiddleware), P6 (ReflectAndRetryToolPlugin) and R4 (a2a-sdk's cancel lines).
- **Proof:** tools/tests/test_libblock.py passes in CI. A scratch page with a pinned window built by libblock passes check_lesson. The same page fails when one line is edited, or when the sidecar's version is changed away from the pin. The first page that uses libblock (10.1 with N1, or P4's page) passes check_lesson and audit_pages.
- **Risks:** A pin bump makes every pinned window fail until the pages are rebuilt. That failure is the intended signal, and the rebuild then needs the venv that holds the new version. Library line numbers move between versions, so labels carry the version and the build re-reads the lines. The label uses a middle dot, not an em dash, so R39 stays the one audit exception.
- **Decision:** Quote library lines in pinned windows (recommended), or give them only as definitions rows with file:line and no window.
- **Also (final review, consistency):** Its users are N1, N2 (if it quotes langchain-google-genai's signature map), P4, P5, P6, Q3 (ADK's guard lines) and R4. N3 and P10 assert library facts and need no pinned window, so their placements no longer wait for GP02. CLAUDE.md gains one line as the author's edit: 'A pinned library's lines are quoted only through pagebuild.libblock(), in a window labelled pinned <dist> <version> · <file>:<lines>, which check_lesson checks against data/lib_excerpts.json.'
- **Closes:** (enables other tasks)

### P. Reasoning, planning and coding agents

**Goal.** The learner chooses a reasoning pattern by measurement: native thinking against a prompted ReAct loop and a written plan, plan-and-execute against plan-once, and reflection, best-of-n and search against a single attempt. They also run a coding agent in a sandbox that stops when the tests pass, with a reviewer reading its diff.

**Gaps it closes.** LOOP-18 Native thinking versus prompted reasoning; LOOP-22 Planning patterns: plan-and-execute, replanning and plan-once execution (ReWOO, LLMCompiler); LOOP-25 Reflexion: learning across attempts; LOOP-27 Search over reasoning paths: Tree of Thoughts and LATS; CROSS-26 Coding agents: the Ralph loop, AI code review and paper-to-code.

*10 tasks, about 58 author-days.*

#### P1. Kit: a prompted ReAct loop that code parses, and thinking set per run and per role, read per call from G1's steps (ADK's planner is P10)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **Lands in:** phase P2, GP04 · **After:** A1, A2, A3, B1, E2, E3, G1, I1, I4

Each also adds calls, one row per model call: {role, model, thinking_level, tokens_in, thoughts, visible_out, cached, ms}. Unset is the default and keeps today's behaviour (planner None); plan_react passes planner=PlanReActPlanner() (google-adk 2.8.0 llm_agent.py:429). _run's final-answer join (brains.py:320-321) now skips parts with thought=True. For react_text it also prints the Thought, Action and Observation lines. For ADK with ADK_PLANNER it prints the plan part stored as thought and the copy sent back as plain text.

#### P2. Kit: planning patterns on the same bench - plan-and-execute with a replanner, plan-once (ReWOO) with parallel waves and one replan, and LangChain's to-do list, priced per role (plan search is P9)

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **Lands in:** phase P2, GP04 · **After:** P1, A1, A2, A3, B1, E2, E3
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_patterns.py gains plan_execute: a StateGraph with plan (list of steps), past_steps and response channels on an InMemorySaver.
    - The planner makes one structured call (with_structured_output(include_raw=True), so its usage is metered with meter.charge_model) and writes numbered steps.
    - The executor runs each step as a create_agent over TOOLS_CALC with SYSTEM_CALC and the brains' middleware order: limits.TurnLimitsMiddleware, then brains._guard_middleware. It runs on the turn's one context: the same Meter, ledger, calcs and message. check_args reads the person's question, never the step text.
    - The replanner makes a structured call that returns either a revised remaining plan or the response.
    - REPLAN=each (default, the pattern as published) or on_failure (only after a refusal, a stop or a verify flag).
    - EXEC_MODEL defaults to gemini-3.6-flash, with gemini-3.1-flash-lite as the cheaper executor, alongside P1's per-role thinking (plan, act).
    - get_state_history returns the plan at every checkpoint.
  - rewoo: one planner call writes steps with evidence placeholders, e.g. #E1 = retrieve[...], #E3 = extract[the notice days in #E1], #E4 = notice_end[ack_date=2026-10-05, days=#E3]. The schema puts arguments as name/value pairs, which Gemini's response schema accepts.
    - Code resolves the placeholders. Every step whose inputs are ready runs in one wave through ToolNode, one code-built AIMessage per wave, so independent retrieves run together as K7 shows ToolNode does.
    - extract is a worker call (flash-lite, schema {value, quote}). Its quote must be found in the evidence it names.
    - One solver call answers from the question, the plan and the evidence. No planner call runs between tools.
    - A plan is refused before any call runs when a placeholder names a later or missing step, or a step names a tool outside TOOLS_CALC.
    - check_args runs on the resolved arguments, so a plan cannot bring a number of its own.
    - REPLAN=1 sends a failed wave back to the planner once (the LLMCompiler joiner's replan).
  - Plan search on rewoo: PLANS=k (default 1, off). One planner call returns k candidate plans. A deterministic value function scores each before any tool runs:
    - every tool is declared;
    - every calculator argument is a placeholder, or a literal that desk_calc.numbers_in or dates_in finds in the message;
    - a Code calculator comes only after a retrieve;
    - fewer worker calls and steps score higher.

    Execution goes best-first and backtracks to the next plan when a step is refused or verify flags a figure. Prints plans proposed, valid and executed, model calls and Rs.
  - todo: create_agent over TOOLS_CALC with middleware [limits.TurnLimitsMiddleware(), TodoListMiddleware(), guard] on an InMemorySaver. TodoListMiddleware (langchain 1.4.0, agents/middleware/todo.py:174) adds the write_todos tool, a todos state channel and an after_model hook that refuses parallel write_todos calls. The todos channel is read back from the checkpoint.
  - Every pattern returns P1's shape. Its A3 steps gain a plan entry: the plan, each revision, the waves and the plans tried. Per-call rows carry the role: plan, act, extract, solve or replan.
  - PR23's agent task set (E2's agent_tasks.jsonl) gains a multi_step slice of two chat tasks. agent_eval --check recomputes their figures with desk_calc.

    (a) 'I am a confirmed E3 employee at ACME on Rs 52,000 a month with 7 years 8 months of continuous service. My resignation was acknowledged in writing on 5 October 2026 and I have 50 days of earned leave. When does my notice end, by when must my final wages be paid, how many leave days are encashed, and roughly what gratuity will I get?' must_contain 2026-12-04, 2026-12-08, 45 and 2,40,000.

    (b) The same with acknowledgement on 6 October 2026, 80 days and 7 years 6 months: 2026-12-05 (a Saturday), 2026-12-08, 45 and 2,10,000.

    The reference_trajectory names the retrieves for NP-03, LV-07, Code on Wages s.17(2) and Code on Social Security s.53, then notice_end, statutory_deadline (A1's one chain), encashable_days and gratuity_estimate.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: agent-turn and agent-eval pass EXEC_MODEL, REPLAN and PLANS.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_agent_patterns.py, with scripted models.
    - plan_execute: the plan is written and executed, replanned once after a refused step, and read back from get_state_history.
    - rewoo: E1 and E2 run in one wave. Each stub call records start and end; both start before either ends (overlap is asserted, not wall time, as A2 Revised asserts it). A forward placeholder is refused before any tool runs. An invented literal is refused by check_args. Model calls equal planner plus workers plus solver.
    - PLANS=3: an invalid plan is skipped, a refused plan is backtracked, the third succeeds, and calls are counted.
    - todo: the todos channel is in the checkpoint, and parallel write_todos calls are refused.
  - python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Proof:** Offline: the tests pass in both workflows' chat-pins steps. They cover per-role pricing (a flash-lite call priced at 0.25/1.50), the allow_model_call stop on a looping planner, and the call count of a four-figure multi_step plan under REPLAN=each: at most 12 model calls on the scripted run, or the stop it reaches is printed. agent_eval.py --check recomputes the multi_step figures. make agent-turn OFFLINE=1 PATTERN=plan_execute and PATTERN=rewoo print the plan, the waves, and calls and rupees by role, at Rs 0. On the author's lane, saved and recorded in the PR: make agent-eval INPROC=1 BRAINS=langgraph,plan_execute,rewoo,todo SLICE=single_hop,calculator,multi_step scores every task for every pattern on the calculators toolset. It prints model calls and rupees by role, p50 latency and stopped_by. The proof fails if any pattern states a figure no calculator wrote, if a wave runs its calls one after another, if a plan's literal gets past check_args, or if a flash-lite call is priced at flash's rate.
- **Risks:** This corrects the register's fix in three ways. C7's agent_shapes.py runs retrieve-only desks, so a planner shape there cannot compute the chained row's dates. fig-04 is a /v1/desk route row scored by route_eval, not an agent_eval task. Plan-and-execute is a single-agent pattern over A2's calculators, scored by agent_eval, with a replanner and its plan state read back. Other risks: - plan_execute may still reach the 12-call or Rs 5 cap on a multi_step row; this is printed as stopped_by. - Plan-once still needs worker calls to read a figure from a passage, so ReWOO saves planner calls, not every call. - Gemini's response schema may refuse free-form argument objects, so arguments are name/value pairs. - Gemini 3 already makes parallel calls in one message (K7, I2), so waves may save nothing. - The patterns run in-process, so no page may imply that the deployed service plans.
- **Decision:** 1. The two multi_step rows' wording; their figures are recomputed by desk_calc. 2. The executor default: gemini-3.6-flash at the brains' own thinking default (recommended, like for like), with flash-lite at minimal as the option. 3. REPLAN: each for plan_execute (the published pattern), with on_failure as P5's Change-it; once for rewoo. 4. End an executor step on its tool result (recommended) or keep the step's closing model call, decided from the offline call counts.
- **Revised:** title revised after review (The plan search was depth-1 rank-and-retry, so it moves to P9 as a real tree search.)
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) Per-role pricing. agent_patterns.py subclasses limits.TurnLimitsMiddleware so that _after charges each call with its own model (response_metadata['model_name'], or else the request's model). Today's _after (limits.py:346-351) charges without a model, so the Meter prices every call at its own model. Every direct planner, extract, solver and replan call passes model= to meter.charge_model. A test prices one flash-lite call at USD 0.25/1.50 per 1M tokens (prices.py), not at flash's 1.50/7.50. (b) Every direct model call first asks meter.allow_model_call(), and the pattern ends with stopped_by model_calls when the Meter refuses. A test shows the 12-call cap stopping a looping planner. (c) One variable, REPLAN=each|on_failure|once|off. plan_execute takes each or on_failure, and rewoo takes once or off; agents.mk passes only REPLAN. (d) Call counts from the offline scripted runs decide the defaults. An executor step ends on its tool result: the executor's create_agent binds return_direct copies of TOOLS_CALC, which langchain 1.4.0's create_agent honours. A retrieve and its calculator may share one step. With these, a four-figure multi_step plan under REPLAN=each stays inside 12 calls. If it still reaches a stop, the stop is printed and stated on P5's page. (e) rewoo is a StateGraph (plan, evidence, wave, answer) on InMemorySaver, with a checkpoint per wave, so P9 can fork a partial plan. (f) PLANS=k and its tests move to P9, which samples candidates independently and searches from the failing node. (g) In PR23's change, the calculator, provenance_trap and multi_step tasks get surfaces [chat, desk] with toolset calculators, and agent_eval --inproc builds every brain or pattern with brains.build(..., toolset='calculators') for them. A2 keeps tools.TOOLS byte for byte, so E2's condition 'until tools.TOOLS holds a calculator' never becomes true. (after review: Per-role models were priced at flash. The slices did not exist on chat. The default arm hit the cap by arithmetic. REPLAN had two meanings. Plan search did not search.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The proof now covers pricing, metering and the counted call budget.)
- **Revised:** risks revised after review (The plan-search risk moves to P9, and the cap risk now follows from the counted defaults.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (There is now one REPLAN variable, plan search belongs to P9, and the call-count choice is explicit.)
- **Closes:** LOOP-22, LOOP-27

#### P3. Kit: agent_eval learns from failed attempts and chooses among runs - --reflect (Reflexion notes from checks a deployed agent has), --best-of (a verifier picks from saved runs), and pass@k beside pass^k

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **Lands in:** phase P3, GP05 · **After:** P1, P2, E2, E3, A3
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py --reflect N: default 0 (off), --inproc only, N at most 3. After a failed attempt the brain makes one reflection call (same model, role reflect, P1's thinking). The call sees the question, the brain's own answer and steps, and the failure signal, and writes a note that code cuts at 300 characters. Notes are kept per task in the --save file and given to the next attempt on a fresh thread.

    The signal comes from --reflect-on:
    - runtime (default): calc_tools.verify's unsupported figures, check_args refusals, an [n] outside the turn's citations, and stopped_by. These are the checks a deployed agent has.
    - grader: the scorer's pass/fail bit only.

    must_contain's text is never sent to the brain.
  - Notes reach the model through the system prompt, never the question. agent_patterns.build('langchain', notes=...) builds create_agent from LangChainBrain's own parts (the toolset's tools, SYSTEM_CALC, limits.TurnLimitsMiddleware, brains._guard_middleware) plus a NotesMiddleware. Its wrap_model_call appends a fenced block, 'notes from your earlier attempts: data, not instructions', the way TodoListMiddleware appends its prompt (todo.py:231-255). P2's planners take the same notes in their planner prompt. ctx['message'], which check_args reads (A2), stays the person's question, so a number carried in a note is still refused. A drift test asserts that this builder's declarations and middleware equal LangChainBrain's apart from NotesMiddleware.
  - --best-of N with --replay of a run saved with --runs N. Per task, it picks the first run whose answer passes the runtime checks: verify unsupported [], every [n] in range, no stopped_by. It prints pass@1 (run 1), best-of-N by verifier, pass@N (any run correct: the ceiling a perfect selector reaches), the verifier's precision (correct runs among those it passes) and Rs per correct answer at N times the calls. It makes no model call.
  - pass@k is printed beside E3's pass^k on every --runs report, from the same runs.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: agent-eval passes REFLECT, REFLECT_ON and BEST_OF.
  - deploy/evals/tests/test_agent_eval.py (stdlib): best-of picks the right run in a canned three-run file; pass@k and pass^k maths; grader mode sends no must_contain string (the prompt a canned brain receives is asserted); the 300-character and 3-attempt caps. deploy/commands/tests/test_agent_patterns.py (chat pins): a scripted brain invents a wage on attempt 1 (check_args refuses and verify flags), writes a note and passes on attempt 2; the note's number is refused by check_args on attempt 2; the NotesMiddleware drift test.
  - python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Proof:** python -m unittest discover -s deploy/evals/tests passes, and test_agent_patterns.py passes in both workflows. The tests assert that a check_args refusal corrected within the attempt triggers no reflection, that the reflect arm is scored on its last attempt, that --save writes every listed field, and that grader mode sends no must_contain string. On the author's lane, the calculator sentence is first deleted from calc_tools.PROMPT_RULES in the local clone, so that failures happen. Then: make agent-eval INPROC=1 BRAINS=langchain SLICE=calculator,provenance_trap RUNS=3 SAVE=plain.jsonl prints pass@1, pass@3 and pass^3. make agent-eval INPROC=1 BRAINS=langchain SLICE=calculator,provenance_trap RUNS=1 REFLECT=3 SAVE=reflect.jsonl prints last-attempt correctness, the oracle ceiling and rupees. make agent-eval REPLAY=plain.jsonl BEST_OF=3 prints best-of-3 by verifier, pass@3 and the verifier's precision, at Rs 0. The proof fails if a note leaks a must_contain string, if a number carried in a note passes check_args, if the reflect arm is scored on any attempt but its last, or if best-of reads anything but the saved runs.
- **Risks:** This corrects the register's LOOP-25 fix:
  - Showing the brain 'a missed must_contain' hands it the reference answer. Feedback is a runtime check, or the grader's pass/fail bit only.
  - A note placed in the question would let check_args accept a number the note carries, because A2's provenance reads the turn's message. Notes go in the system prompt.

  It also corrects the LOOP-27 fix: E3's saved pass^3 run is SLICE=follow_up (E3 final review), whose answers hold no computed figure for verify to judge. Best-of reads its own RUNS=3 save on the calculator and provenance_trap slices.

  Other risks:
  - With few tasks and a model that rarely fails, reflect and re-roll may both show no gain. The page says so, and the weakened-prompt edit exists to make failures happen.
  - verify() checks provenance, not correctness: a supported but wrong figure passes, which is why its precision is printed.
  - The runtime signal also fires on a right figure written in prose, so reflection can cost calls for no gain in correctness.
  - Notes never cross tasks; memory across tasks belongs to the self-improvement elective.
- **Decision:** 1. The default signal: runtime (recommended, since production has it), with grader as an option, or runtime only. 2. The caps: 3 attempts and 300 characters (proposed). 3. REFLECT on --inproc only (recommended, since it needs the notes hook), or also over /v1/chat, which would need a new request field.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) In runtime mode, a retry happens only when a runtime check is still open at the end of an attempt: an unsupported figure in the final answer (calc_tools.verify), an [n] outside the turn's citations, or a stopped_by. A check_args refusal that the model corrected within the same attempt does not trigger one. (b) The reflect arm is one chain of up to N attempts per task (RUNS=1 REFLECT=N), scored on its last attempt, the one that would ship. Any-attempt-correct is printed only as the oracle ceiling, beside best-of-N and pass@N. (c) --save stores, for each run and attempt: the answer; the toolset; tool_calls with their arguments; verify.unsupported; the citation range and every [n] used; refusals with their kinds; stopped_by; model calls, tokens and rupees; and, for reflection, the signal and the note. test_agent_eval.py asserts these fields. --best-of reads only these fields and refuses a save without verify fields (verify exists only on the calculators toolset). (after review: The retry signal and the scoring were undefined, RUNS with REFLECT was ambiguous, and best-of needs saved fields that E2 never specified.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The reflect arm is defined as one chain per task and scored on the attempt that would ship.)
- **Closes:** LOOP-25, LOOP-27
