#### R6. A partner's agent: a caller-side A2A contract test pinned and broken on the lane, and what holds for any partner organisation's agent (12.3, with one sentence in 12.2)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P4, PR58 (an optional Level 5 step with the contract test and the partner rows, plus one sentence in 12.2) · **After:** J6, C6, J8, J10, J5, H5 (soft), R7
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/smoke/a2a_contract.py (new): a caller-side contract test for any A2A agent. It takes four arguments. URL. TOKEN_CMD: a command that prints a bearer token; by default an ID token minted as documind-ui-sa for URL, as smoke_agent.token_as does, or an OAuth 2.0 JWT for an OAuth-protected agent. CONTRACT: a JSON file. PIN: a file. The test checks the card at /.well-known/agent-card.json (refused without a token where the contract says so), the interface's binding and protocol version, the skill ids the contract names, one probe that must complete with an expected pattern, one probe that must be refused, and each call's latency and response bytes. On the first run, PIN records the card's version and a hash of each skill's description. Later runs fail on drift, for example 'version drift: COMMIT -> new' or 'skill drift: corpus_inventory description changed'. A 401 or 403 is reported as 'refused at the door', never as a contract failure.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_agent.py imports its token, http and send helpers from a2a_contract.py, so one A2A client serves both the kit's gate and the caller's contract.
  - deploy/evals/peer_contract.json (new): the kit's peer as a partner would pin it. It holds J6's skill ids (cited_answers, corpus_inventory, processing_cost), the JSONRPC binding and the protocol version J8 reconciles, the gratuity probe with smoke_agent.py's expected pattern, and the zeta refusal probe.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk: a2a-contract [URL=] [TOKEN_CMD=] [CONTRACT=evals/peer_contract.json] [PIN=$HOME/peer-pin.json], defaulting to the lane's documind-agent; added to .PHONY and deploy/mk/README.md.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_a2a_contract.py (agent-pins CI venv): the contract passes against the in-process peer; a renamed skill fails with skill drift; a changed AGENT_VERSION fails with version drift; a card without the pinned binding fails the protocol check; a bad token reports 'refused at the door'. commands/tests/test_smoke_agent_expectations.py stays green.
  - python tools/kit_index.py. Forced rebuild of 12.3, because smoke_agent.py's quoted checks move.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3 Trace the implemented A2A peer and its permissions (rewritten by J8, PR58) = corrected M7 'A2A peer and multi-agent handoff [12.3 + 12.4]'**, Level 5, a new optional step before Verify: 'A partner's agent: the contract you pin, and what changes on another cloud': Runs make a2a-contract PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID; the first run writes the pin. Change it: prints documind-agent's AGENT_VERSION, sets it to another value with gcloud run services update documind-agent --region=REGION --update-env-vars=AGENT_VERSION=bumped (no build), re-runs the same command, then sets the printed value back and runs it once more. *The learner sees:* Every check with its latency and bytes: the card, the binding and protocol version, the three pinned skills, a completed gratuity task and the zeta refusal. After the bump: 'version drift: COMMIT -> bumped' and FAIL, with no task sent. After the restore: PASS. The point: you do not own another company's agent, so you pin its contract and re-run the test on a schedule.
  - **12.3 (J8, PR58) = corrected M7 'A2A peer and multi-agent handoff [12.3 + 12.4]'**, The same optional step: a table built at build time from a dated facts file in the lesson folder: Reads the table. *The learner sees:* This lane compared with a partner's agent on Bedrock AgentCore Runtime and on Azure AI Foundry Agent Service. Each fact carries its source and the date it was read. The rows: (1) the A2A protocol and card path each one serves; (2) how you authenticate to theirs: an OAuth 2.0 bearer token through TOKEN_CMD, with SigV4 named as needing request signing; (3) how theirs authenticates to yours: a Workload Identity Federation pool with an AWS, Azure or OIDC provider and an attribute condition, on the pattern of terraform/wif.tf's GitHub provider (quoted verbatim), impersonating a partner account bound as invoker on documind-agent only; this is the mechanism 14.1 builds for GitHub; (4) the network path and egress, priced per 1,000 tasks from the bytes the contract run measured and a dated price; (5) the DPDP Act's s.16 on transfer outside India, quoted from evals/corpus/acme/dpdp_act_2023.md:529-535, with its in-force line from shared/desk_law.py; (6) what the partner keeps: task history and contextId sessions, outside your erasure path (H5); (7) who owns the version: they do, so you rely on your pin and the scheduled contract run.
  - **12.2 Deploy MCP and verify authorized access (rewritten by J5, PR57) = corrected M5 'MCP deploy and hardening [12.2]'**, Level 5, one sentence beside the identity-propagation options: Reads the sentence. *The learner sees:* An MCP host is another organisation's agent too. Gemini CLI's model provider receives every tool result the bridge relays (acme's passages), so the host is a processor to name in the tenant's DPDP record, just as the A2A partner is.
- **Proof:** CI: test_a2a_contract.py's cases pass, and test_smoke_agent_expectations.py stays green. On the author's lane, make a2a-contract writes the pin and passes. After the AGENT_VERSION bump the same command fails with 'version drift', and after the restore it passes; the runs are captured and labelled with identifiers as placeholders. 12.3's build asserts four things: every row of the facts file has a source URL and an access date; the DPDP quote matches the corpus file line for line; the in-force line comes from desk_law.in_force_line; and the wif.tf excerpt is verbatim through block(). python pagekit/check_lesson.py 12.3 and 12.2 pass, as do the audit_pages runs. Optional author probe: make a2a-contract URL=<an AgentCore or Foundry A2A agent> TOKEN_CMD=<its OAuth token> passes the card and protocol checks, recorded in the PR. The proof fails if the version bump does not fail the contract, or if any table row lacks a dated source.
- **Risks:** AgentCore and Foundry facts change quickly, so every row is dated and J9's final Module 12 pass re-reads them. No AWS or Azure account sits on a learner's lane, so the cross-cloud half is documented unless the author's probe runs, and the page labels it that way. SigV4 signs each request, so it cannot be supplied through TOKEN_CMD; the page names it and shows OAuth 2.0 as the AgentCore path a2a_contract supports. The AGENT_VERSION bump creates a new documind-agent revision, so the cell prints the old value first and the restore is exact. Moving smoke_agent.py's helpers shifts 12.3's quoted lines (a forced rebuild). The DPDP quote must be verbatim, and its in-force line comes from shared/desk_law.py, never a typed date. 12.3's weight: this is an optional step. PR slicing: one 12.3 PR after PR58, together with R4; the 12.2 sentence rides in it as a pointer edit.
- **Decision:** (1) Placement: an optional Level 5 step in 12.3, beside the card and its version (recommended), or in 12.4. (2) Whether the author runs a dated probe of one AgentCore or Foundry A2A agent with make a2a-contract (recommended if an account is at hand; otherwise the table is labelled 'documented, not run'). (3) a2a-contract stays a target and does not gate make handoff (recommended).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) a2a_contract resolves the card relative to URL (URL + '/.well-known/agent-card.json'), with an optional CARD_URL override. ADK's to_a2a can mount the card under an rpc_path prefix (agent_to_a2a.py:207-209), and a hosted partner agent may do the same. test_a2a_contract.py adds a prefixed case against an in-process to_a2a app built with rpc_path. (b) The DPDP row labels desk_law.in_force_line('dpdp_act_2023') as the Act's commencement note. That line speaks of the Act as a whole, or of its rights provisions once IN_FORCE['dpdp_rights'] is set (shared/desk_law.py:251-262); it does not speak of s.16 in Chapter IV. (after review: The contract assumed the card sits at the host root, and the in-force line describes different provisions from s.16.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (a) Row (4) names Private Service Connect, Cloud VPN and Cloud Interconnect explicitly, each with a dated source in the facts file. (b) Row (5) labels the in-force line as the Act's commencement note. (c) The 12.2 sentence rides PR58 as a pointer edit, after PR57 (J5) (G-decision 9 (a)). (after review: Private connectivity was only implied, and the 12.2 sentence broke one-lesson-per-PR.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: J6, C6, J8, J10, J5, H5 (soft), R7. R7 is added. (after review: The optional step adds to 12.3, whose budget R7 sets.)
- **Also (the author's decision, 7 October 2026: one cloud for now):** Under G-decision 11 (b), the Level 5 step keeps the caller-side contract test (pinned, broken with an AGENT_VERSION bump, restored) and the rows that hold for any partner organisation. It drops the Bedrock AgentCore and Azure AI Foundry columns, SigV4, and the optional author probe against an AgentCore or Foundry agent, and names no other cloud's platform. MULTI-15's cross-cloud half is deferred until a second cloud is in scope.
- **Also (final review, MULTI-15):** Lesson, 12.3 Level 5 optional step: add a row 'Residency: where the partner's agent runs and keeps its task history'. Under G-decision 11 (b) it names no other cloud's platform.
  - It shows the lane side, from a dated capture of make agent-inventory PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID TENANT=globex: M9's residency line, with documind-agent on REGION and Gemini on global, shown as placeholders.
  - One sentence follows. A handoff to a partner running outside India sends an 'in' tenant's words across the border, which is the case row (5)'s s.16 governs, so the tenant's DPDP record names that partner and its region.

  Proof: 12.3's build asserts that the row's lane cell comes from agent_inventory's residency output rather than typed text.
- **Closes:** MULTI-15

#### R7. Plan the minutes before writing: a per-step budget for 12.3 and 12.4, and for 10.1, 10.4 and 13.1-13.3, across every task that adds to them, the go-deeper set, and whether the composition and managed-platform levels become their own corrected M7 lessons

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **Lands in:** phase P0, GP01 · **After:** C4, C7, J8, I7, M1
- **Kit:**
  - plan/ only, with no deploy/ change. A page plan for 12.4, written before PR61's content and the shared 12.4 content PR. It gives minutes per step for C4's five levels, C7's composition primitives, Q1's Change-it, Q2's teams and levels table, Q3's shapes, map and Change-it, Q4's handoffs step, Q5's board, R1's boundaries step, R2's loops and deadlocks, R3's drill and Level 5 cells, and R5's labelling step and MAST table. The core must sum to at most 90 minutes.
  - The same for 12.3, before the content of PR58 and PR65: J8's rewrite, I7's Agent Engine level, N4's code action, N5's surfaces table, R4's lab, R6's partner step, S6's capacity row and S9's registry sub-level.

(b) Whether I7's level, with N4, N5 and S9, becomes M7's 'Google's managed agent platform' page instead of a 12.3 level. A split's manifest entry, counts and demo map land with its page (M1's rule), and tools/check_counts.py verifies the resulting count.

Each plan gives minutes per step, places every named task's step in the core or the go-deeper part, and records the two split decisions. The timed rehearsals in PR95 compare the plan with the clock; a page over 90 minutes moves its next go-deeper candidate out before merge. check_counts.py passes for whatever count the split decisions produce.

Authors of Q and R tasks may resist moving their steps to go-deeper; the plan is agreed once, before writing, rather than after rehearsal.

Recommended: split only if the 12.4 plan still exceeds 90 minutes with the go-deeper set moved out. (2) Make I7's level, with N4, N5 and S9, the corrected M7 managed-platform page (recommended if 12.3's plan exceeds 90 minutes), or keep it in 12.3.

### S. Evaluation, operations, safety and governance

**Goal.** The learner validates judges against people, scores live agent traffic, sizes capacity, threat-models and governs agents, and handles Hindi and Hinglish. The final review's slot definitions add incident remediation, production gotchas, the capstone's agent lane and rag-api's attack set (S10-S14).

**Gaps it closes.** CROSS-13 LLM judges validated against people; CROSS-14 Simulation, red-teaming and online evaluation; CROSS-20 Deployment, runtime and capacity; CROSS-23 Governance: agent inventory, registry and discovery, gateway policy and audit; CROSS-25 Agent security review and threat modelling; CROSS-28 Indian languages and code-mixed input across the agent.

*14 tasks, about 78 author-days.*

#### S1. Capture the frozen judge set on the author's lane, and commission people's labels on it plus the Hindi and Hinglish rows the other tasks need (an extension of E4's writer commission)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **Lands in:** phase P5, GP06 · **After:** E4, S2, A2, I1
- **Kit:**
  - Run S2's deploy/evals/build_judge_set.py --capture once on the author's lane. The run is dated, with the revision kept as a placeholder. It asks two sets of questions: the golden questions (evals/golden.jsonl, 64 rows) and about 45 new ones written by E4's fluent writer, about half in Devanagari Hindi and half in Hinglish. Each new question names an English twin where one exists. Every question goes through /v1/query on two arms: the live revision, and a no-traffic candidate with GENERATOR_MODEL=gemini-3.1-flash-lite (7.3's make candidate, with the tag removed afterwards), so the set contains natural failures. A third of the questions also go through /v1/chat with brain langchain on agent_tools=calculators (A2), because S4's sampled judge scores agent answers. Each answer is frozen with the full text of the chunks it cited (judge.enrich_context, the fix that stopped a quote-only judge reading 0.25 in 7.2), its arm and its source.
  - build_judge_set.py --pick draws three things. First, 90 pointwise rows: 45 English, about 22 Hindi and about 23 Hinglish, stratified by language, arm, source and golden shape. Second, 20 pairs: one golden question answered by both revisions. Third, 15 rows that both people pass, to receive padded copies. --emit writes label sheets that carry no judge score.
  - E4's two labellers, one fluent in Hindi and Hinglish, label every row independently under a one-page guide committed as deploy/evals/judge_labels.GUIDE.md. Each pointwise row gets two labels: groundedness yes/no (every claim is supported by the frozen context; a refusal counts as grounded) and answers-the-question yes/no. Each pair gets A better, B better or tie. Each padded copy gets 'adds no claim and loses none'. Where a language's natural failures fall below a third of its rows, the fluent writer writes one unsupported sentence into a copy of a grounded answer and flags it constructed: true.
  - A third reader adjudicates every disagreement, and must be fluent for Hindi and Hinglish rows. build_judge_set.py --merge-labels joins the sheets. The people's kappa per rubric and per language is computed with S2's evals/agreement.py. It is recorded in deploy/evals/judge_labels.REVIEWED.json with pseudonymous reader ids (L1, L2, A1), dates and counts, in the shape of the REVIEWED record that E4's final-review note defines. The record holds no names, emails or lane identifiers.
  - The same commission also writes rows for S5 and S8. Four Hindi and Hinglish agent tasks with English twins, for E2's evals/agent_tasks.jsonl: a '52 hazaar, 7 saal 8 mahine' gratuity question, a Devanagari notice question acknowledged on '6 अक्टूबर 2026', a Hinglish follow-up pair, and a Hinglish gratuity question that gives no wage. Three attack rows for F10's evals/adversarial/attacks.jsonl, two Hinglish and one Devanagari. Two Hinglish persona cards for S5's evals/sim_personas.jsonl. A fluent review of S8's Hindi scale words, month names and negation words.
  - Run build_judge_set.py --check, then python tools/leakscan.py on every new file. Commit deploy/evals/judge_labels.jsonl, the guide and the REVIEWED record. Then run python tools/kit_index.py, then --check.
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/build_judge_set.py --check passes on the committed judge_labels.jsonl and judge_labels.REVIEWED.json. It fails in each of these cases: a language has fewer than 20 pointwise rows; fewer than a third of a language's rows fail a rubric by the adjudicated label; a row lacks either reader's label; a hi or hinglish row's two labels are not both from fluent readers; a disagreement lacks an adjudication; a dev question equals a test question under route_eval.normalise; a judge-set question equals a routes.jsonl test-split row, a row in a tuning file, or an SFT row's question (compared on the text after 'Question:'); the people's kappa on a rubric falls below the bar the author sets. Overlaps with golden-sourced routes.jsonl dev rows and with E2's agent_tasks are reported as shared questions, not failures. All 65 golden questions are routes.jsonl dev rows today. tools/leakscan.py passes on every new file.
- **Risks:** Writer and labeller time is two to three weeks elapsed, and it starts only after the capture, so this task sits on S3's critical path. Folding it into E4's commission shares the brief. A second fluent labeller adds about two reader-days for the Indic rows. Hindi questions against English documents may retrieve poorly and come back as refusals, which a groundedness judge always passes. Constructed negatives may therefore be needed, and S3 reports natural and constructed rows separately. The frozen answers age as the kit changes. That matters little, because the set measures the judge; it is recaptured only when the rubric, the resolved metric or the judge's template hash changes. The captured context is the kit's own synthetic corpus and must stay free of lane identifiers. Labelling 90 rows, 20 pairs and about 20 padded candidates is about two reader-days per reader.
- **Decision:** Fold this into E4's commission (recommended) or commission it separately. Set the people's agreement bar per rubric. Recommended: a Cohen's kappa of 0.6 or more per rubric, reported per language; below it, the guide is rewritten and that rubric relabelled. E4 keeps 0.8 for route labels, which are crisper. Hindi and Hinglish rows get two fluent labellers and a third fluent adjudicator (recommended; the author alone does not adjudicate). Take 90 pointwise rows, or a 60-row floor if the budget is short.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) The golden count is computed at capture time and never typed. evals/golden.jsonl holds 65 rows today: lookup 34, join 11, isolation 11, refusal 8, version 1. (b) A second labeller fluent in Hindi and Hinglish labels the hi and hinglish rows, so every Indic row has two fluent labels. The adjudicator is a third fluent person. judge_labels.REVIEWED.json records reader ids per language, and build_judge_set.py --check fails a hi or hinglish row whose two labels are not both from readers marked fluent. (c) Each /v1/chat calculator answer is frozen with its 'Worked out in code' lines (the turn's calculator results) in its context, as S4's export does, so a figure a calculator worked out is not read as unsupported. (d) judge_labels.GUIDE.md is written from the metric template the pinned SDK resolves, quoting that rubric and its template hash. judge.pointwise_metrics() resolves to FULFILLMENT, INSTRUCTION_FOLLOWING or QUESTION_ANSWERING_QUALITY, and to_frame's instruction adds 'using only the retrieved context'. A change of the resolved metric means relabelling. (e) --pick pads about 20 gate-passing candidates in the same labelling round, and keeps the 15 that both readers pass after --merge-labels. The contamination rule is S2's amended one. (after review: The golden set has 65 rows, not 64; one non-fluent labeller skews Indic kappa; calculator figures read as unsupported; the rubrics differed; padding was picked before any label existed.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Under the old check S1's proof could never pass, because every golden question is in routes.jsonl.)
- **Revised:** risks revised after review (Adds the second fluent labeller and the recapture triggers.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (The second label on Indic rows must also come from a fluent reader.)
- **Also (final review: slot definition, corrected M4 'error analysis and adversarial evals'):** In the same labelling round, each reader gives every pointwise row that either rubric label fails two more fields:
  1. a one-line note, written before they read the class list (open coding);
  2. one class from S2's triage.py rag taxonomy: retrieval, version, omission, unsupported, overreach, isolation, infra or row.

  The adjudicator settles every class disagreement.
  - judge_labels.jsonl gains note_l1, note_l2, class_l1, class_l2 and class_adj. Constructed rows carry class unsupported with constructed true, and are counted apart.
  - judge_labels.REVIEWED.json gains the class counts per language, and the readers' class agreement through agreement.cohen_kappa.
  - The one-page guide gains one example per class, from the kit's own corpus.
  - build_judge_set.py --check fails a failing pointwise row without both classes and an adjudicated class.

  This costs about half a reader-day more per reader, inside the commission's second drop.

  If the class fields are late, 7.4 prints the author's classes labelled 'one reader: no agreement figure' and withholds its class-agreement line.
- **Closes:** CROSS-13, CROSS-28

#### S2. Kit: per-row judge ratings, a judge that rates frozen rows, and its agreement with people measured: kappa, true-pass and false-pass rates, order bias and length bias

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **Lands in:** phase P2, GP03 · **After:** E1, E2, I1, I3, E4 (soft: whichever of E4 and S2 lands second wires --kappa to agreement.cohen_kappa)
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/judge.py: --frame FILE judges frozen rows, in the shape collect() returns (id, shape, question, context, response, must_contain), instead of asking the lane. With --baseline-frame FILE it judges pairs. Both go through the existing to_frame() and evaluate(). --save-rows FILE writes result.metrics_table row by row: id, metric, score and explanation, plus pairwise_choice for a pair. Today evaluate() reads that table only for the by-shape mean (judge.py:241-245) and returns only the summary (:247), so agreement cannot be computed (the register's verifier confirmed this). --pairwise-only drops the two pointwise metrics, for the order-swap run. Every run prints, beside each metric, its calibration on this lane from evals/reports/judge_agree.json: ADVISORY, NOT CALIBRATED, or 'not measured: run make judge-agree'. The lines 7.2 quotes and the strings E1 keeps byte for byte stay as they are; check_lesson names any forced rebuild of 7.2, 7.3 or 17.3.
  - deploy/evals/agreement.py (new, stdlib) holds: cohen_kappa(a, b) over any label set; a 2x2 confusion table; the true-pass and false-pass rates with route_eval.wilson() intervals (route_eval.py:297); and flips(run_a, run_b) for a pairwise run repeated with the two answers swapped. E4's build_routes.py --kappa calls cohen_kappa instead of carrying its own copy. Route labels, judge labels and any later error-analysis labelling then share one implementation.
  - deploy/evals/build_judge_set.py (new):
    - --capture, --pick and --emit as S1 runs them.
    - --emit swapped writes each pair with its two answers exchanged.
    - --emit padded appends two content-free sentences (a restatement and a courtesy line) to a copy of an answer both readers passed.
    - --merge-labels joins the readers' sheets.
    - --check enforces the schema and per-language counts, both labels plus an adjudication on every disagreement, and at least a third failing rows per rubric and language. It also checks that the dev and test halves are stratified by language and label, that no question is equal under route_eval.normalise across the halves, and that no question equals a routes.jsonl row, an E2 agent_tasks.jsonl task or a file under evals/sft. That last check keeps a labelled row out of every set something is tuned on.
    - --selftest runs on canned sheets.
  - deploy/evals/judge_agree.py (new, stdlib) has six modes:
    - people: kappa per rubric and language, and the adjudications.
    - judge RUN: per rubric and language, the confusion against the adjudicated label; the true-pass rate, false-pass rate and kappa(judge, people), each with an interval; natural and constructed rows apart; and the verdict against the calibration bar. Groundedness is scored on every row. The second metric's 1-5 score is cut at CUT, chosen on the dev half and reported on the test half.
    - gate: on figure rows, run_eval.contains() (run_eval.py:337) on must_contain, beside the judge and the people.
    - swap RUN_A RUN_B: the pairwise verdicts that followed the position rather than the answer, with an interval.
    - pad RUN: score changes on padded copies the people said changed nothing.
    - tool-use ADK_RESULTS AGENT_EVAL_RUN: I3's rubric tool-use verdict per case, against agent_eval's computed trajectory-and-argument verdict on the same task.

    It writes evals/reports/judge_agree.json, which judge.py's calibration line reads. --selftest.
  - deploy/mk/evaluation.mk (new, Module 7) holds two targets. judge-calibrate runs judge.py --frame on build_judge_set's emitted frames in ~/judge-venv, the way 7.2 runs make judge, with SWAP=1 and PAD=1. judge-agree takes RUN=, CUT=, PEOPLE=1 and TOOLUSE=1, and is stdlib at Rs 0. Both go in the Makefile's .PHONY, plus a mk/README.md row.
  - Check the pinned google-cloud-aiplatform[evaluation]==2.1.0 source for two things. First, whether the GenAI evals client exposes AutoraterConfig's autorater_model, sampling_count and flip_enabled. 1.153.1's vertexai/_genai/_evals_utils.py:266-283 maps all three, while 7.2 states that the EvalTask path it uses pins no judge model. Second, whether vertexai.preview.evaluation.autorater_utils.evaluate_autorater exists. Record each fact as a row in I1's facts table. If both are present, judge.py's existing --judge-model reaches the judge model, and judge-agree prints Vertex's own agreement figures beside the stdlib ones. If not, both print 'off in this SDK'.
  - Tests, picked up by unittest discover in .github/workflows/checks.yml and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml:
    - evals/tests/test_agreement.py: kappa 0.40 on the worked 50-item table of 20, 5, 10 and 15.
    - test_judge_agree.py: canned swap, pad, cut and tool-use runs.
    - test_judge_frame.py: --frame and --save-rows against a fake metrics table, in CI's shared interpreter, which already installs pandas.
    - One case in E2's test_agent_eval.py: --vertex never changes agent_eval's exit status. The judge explains; a computed check decides.
  - Add deploy/UNOWNED.md rows for the new files until S3's page quotes them. Then run python tools/kit_index.py, then --check.
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/agreement.py --selftest prints kappa 0.40 for the worked table. judge_agree.py --selftest and build_judge_set.py --selftest pass. The test files and the agent_eval case pass in both CI workflows. build_judge_set.py --check fails on a canned set with any of: a dev/test question pair; a judge-set question equal to a routes.jsonl test-split row or to an SFT row's text after 'Question:'; a missing label; an unadjudicated disagreement. It reports a golden-sourced routes.jsonl dev overlap as a shared question without failing. judge_agree's tool-use mode, on a canned adk eval result, scores the same recorded invocations both ways. judge.py --selftest still ends with its four claims, and its calibration line prints 'stale' on a canned judge_agree.json measured for another template hash. On the author's lane, make judge-calibrate writes a rating for every frozen row and prints which judge path ran, and make judge-agree prints per-rubric, per-language rates with Wilson intervals.
- **Risks:** judge.py is quoted by 7.2, 7.3 and 17.3, so the new flags must not move quoted lines; if they do, the PR carries those rebuilds. The metrics_table column names vary by SDK version, so columns are found by suffix, as the by-shape block already does. The pinned SDK may lack the GenAI autorater settings, which leaves self-preference named but not measured. Per-language halves are small, so intervals are wide. I3's ADK result format is needed for the tool-use mode; until I3 lands, that mode is tested on canned results only. This task corrects the register's suggested fix in two places. Judging frozen, labelled answers replaces 'label 40-60 saved rows', because people's labels attach to particular answers and every learner's lane answers differently. Swapping frozen pairs replaces re-collecting from --api-url and --api-b, which would confound order with fresh generations.
- **Decision:** Set the calibration bar that makes a rubric ADVISORY. Recommended: kappa against the adjudicated label of 0.6 or more, and a false-pass rate whose Wilson upper bound is 20% or less, per rubric and language, with at least 10 failing rows; otherwise NOT CALIBRATED or 'too few rows'. Choose the stdlib figures as primary with Vertex's evaluate_autorater as a cross-check (recommended), or the reverse. Publish the frozen set in the learner kit (recommended: it is the kit's own synthetic corpus, it passes leakscan, and 7.4 needs it).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) build_judge_set.py --check scopes its contamination rule to what judge labels could tune. No normalised question appears in both the judge set's dev and test halves. None equals a routes.jsonl test-split row (as E2's --check does), a row in any template or CUT tuning file, or an SFT row compared on the text after 'Question:'; whole-text equality matches 0 of 65, because each SFT user turn carries the system text and the context. Overlaps with golden-sourced routes.jsonl dev rows and with E2's agent_tasks are reported as shared questions, not failures. The printed rule: judge labels never tune the judge, the router or the generator. (b) judge.py gains an explicit second judge path, in new code below the lines 7.2 quotes. That path is either the GenAI client's evals with an autorater config (autorater_model, sampling_count, flip_enabled, which the GenAI evals client maps in vertexai/_genai/_evals_utils.py), or vertexai.preview.evaluation's EvalTask if 2.1.0 accepts autorater_config there. The path is chosen at runtime, and the run prints which path ran. --judge-model reaches the judge only on that path; evaluate()'s vertexai.evaluation.EvalTask path stays as 7.2 quotes it. I1's facts table records which path exists in the pinned 2.1.0. (c) judge_agree's tool-use mode computes agent_eval's trajectory-and-argument verdict on the invocations adk eval recorded (its saved eval result carries each invocation's tool calls), so the rubric and the computed check judge the same path. A separate agent_eval run is context only. (d) Whichever of E4 and S2 lands second wires build_routes.py --kappa to agreement.cohen_kappa. (e) judge_agree.json stores the template hash and judge model it was measured for, and judge.py's calibration line prints 'stale: measured for template X' on a mismatch. (after review: --check failed every golden row; judge.py's EvalTask path cannot pin the judge; the tool-use mode compared two different runs; the E4 order and a stale calibration were unhandled.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The proof now covers the scoped contamination rule, the second path, same-path tool use and stale calibration.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: E1, E2, I1, I3, E4 (soft: whichever of E4 and S2 lands second wires --kappa to agreement.cohen_kappa). (after review: Its order relative to E4 was unstated.)
- **Also (final review: slot definition, corrected M4 'error analysis and adversarial evals'):** Kit: deploy/evals/triage.py (new, stdlib) is created here, beside agreement.py, as the course's one tool for labelling failures by cause. R5 adds its agents classes to this module instead of creating it.

  `--template FILE` writes one line per failing row of the inputs given:
  - a run_eval.py --report file: a record whose pass is false, with its why, stages and cache_hit;
  - a judge.py --reuse collection, joined with this task's --save-rows ratings;
  - judge_labels.jsonl: a pointwise row that either rubric fails by its adjudicated label.

  Each line holds:
  - the row id, shape, language and arm;
  - the evidence the input recorded: the gate's why; whether any of the row's must_retrieve codes appears in the cited context; answerable said against answerable expected; must_contain found; a must_not_contain hit; the HTTP status; the judge's rating and explanation;
  - a suggested class, computed only from that evidence;
  - an empty note and an empty class.

  A row whose gate report and judge collection hold different answers is marked 'two answers' and gets no suggestion.

  `--labels FILE` reads the filled file and refuses an unknown class and a row that did not fail (exit 2). It prints, per class: the count, the lever, the lesson that pulls it, and what can catch it (a gate rule, the judge, or only a person).

  `--taxonomy rag`, the default, has eight classes:
  - retrieval: no must_retrieve code reached the context (make ablate, 5.2-5.4);
  - version: a retired version was cited (the ledger, 4.3);
  - omission: the context held the fact and the answer left it out or refused (packing and the prompt, 6.1-6.2);
  - unsupported: the answer says what its context does not (the judge, 7.4);
  - overreach: an answer to a row the corpus cannot answer (the refusal rule, 6.2);
  - isolation: another tenant's marker (the filter and the roster, 8.1-8.2);
  - infra: a non-200 or a malformed body (the service);
  - row: the golden row or the gate's rule is wrong (7.1's build_golden checks).

  `--people` adds the reader's agreement with the adjudicated classes S1 records, and the suggestion's agreement with them, as k of n and agreement.cohen_kappa.

  deploy/mk/evaluation.mk gains triage (REPORT=, JUDGE=, ROWS=, FRAME=, TEMPLATE=, LABELS=, PEOPLE=1; stdlib, Rs 0), with its .PHONY entry and mk/README.md row. Then run python tools/kit_index.py, and add an UNOWNED.md row until 7.4 quotes the file.

  deploy/evals/tests/test_triage.py covers:
  - the template lists exactly the failing rows of merged inputs;
  - an unknown class and a passing row exit 2;
  - every class prints a lever, a lesson and a catcher;
  - the suggestion rules on canned rows: an answerable row refused with its code in the context suggests omission, and without it, retrieval; an answered unanswerable row suggests overreach;
  - a 'two answers' row gets no suggestion;
  - kappa against canned adjudicated classes.

  Proof adds: python deploy/evals/triage.py --selftest and test_triage.py pass in both CI workflows.

  Effort: about one author-day more.
- **Closes:** CROSS-13

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP12 · **After:** S1, S2, Q7, E1, E3, I3, M1, L1 (L2's pilot)

Remove the UNOWNED rows for the files the page quotes, then run python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 0 (step 1): theory and two interactives: Reads the analogy. Before a new examiner marks board papers alone, the moderator gives them scripts that two senior examiners have already marked. Where the seniors disagreed, the chief examiner's mark stands. An examiner who marks long answers up, or always prefers the first of two scripts, is caught by a padded script and by the same pair handed over in the other order. The learner then picks a rubric and a language in the first panel, and drags the cut slider in the second. *The learner sees:* The first panel is computed at build time from judge_labels.jsonl. It shows the two readers' 2x2 for that rubric and language, their kappa, and each adjudicated disagreement with its context and answer. The second panel replays the author's dated capture. It shows the judge's 2x2 against the adjudicated label as the fulfilment cut moves on the dev half, with the test half's rates fixed at the chosen cut.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 1 (step 2): definitions: Reads the table: label, reader, adjudication, Cohen's kappa, chance agreement, true-pass rate, false-pass rate, dev and test halves, calibration bar, position bias, verbosity bias, self-preference, constructed row. *The learner sees:* Each term next to the file or target that computes it.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 2 (step 3): 'The people first': Runs make judge-agree PEOPLE=1 (Rs 0). Reads three adjudicated rows: one Hinglish, one constructed, and one where the readers split on a refusal. *The learner sees:* Kappa per rubric for English, Hindi and Hinglish, how many disagreements there were and how each was settled, and why agreement is measured against chance.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 3 (step 4): 'The judge on the same rows': Runs make judge-calibrate PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID, which sends the frozen rows through Vertex AI Evaluation from ~/judge-venv, two requests a row. Then runs make judge-agree RUN=evals/reports/judge_cal.jsonl. *The learner sees:* Per rubric and language: the true-pass rate, the false-pass rate and kappa against the people, each with its interval, and ADVISORY or NOT CALIBRATED against the bar. Natural and constructed rows are shown apart. On the figure rows, the gate's computed verdict sits beside the judge's and the people's, showing where a computed check agrees with people more often than the judge does. The run's rupees come from the Evaluation service's line on the bill. Lane values are placeholders, beside the author's capture.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 3 (same step), 'Change it: the fulfilment cut': Runs make judge-agree RUN=... CUT=3, then CUT=4, on the dev half (Rs 0). Picks a cut and runs it once on the test half. The restore is the default CUT. *The learner sees:* False-pass and true-pass rates trading against each other on the dev half, and the test half's rates at the chosen cut. This is why nobody looks at the test half while choosing.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 4 (step 5): 'The judge against itself': Runs make judge-calibrate SWAP=1, which judges the 20 pairs again with the answers in the other order, pairwise only. Then runs make judge-agree swap. Then runs make judge-calibrate PAD=1 and make judge-agree pad. *The learner sees:* How many pairwise verdicts followed the position rather than the answer, with an interval. How often a padded copy scored higher on the second metric although both readers said it added nothing. Self-preference is named but not measured, because every answer and the judge here are Gemini; the people's labels are the reference that would show it. If S2 confirmed the SDK settings, an optional cell pins gemini-3.1-flash-lite as the judge and re-reads agreement, and names flip_enabled as the service's own guard against order bias, at twice the requests.
  - **7.4 Check the LLM judge against people (new; corrected M4 'judge vs people')**, Level 5 (steps 6-8): why, what it costs, the 'does not do yet' box, Verify it yourself, What changed on your lane, footer: Reads the rule and works through the checklist. *The learner sees:* Only computed checks block a release. A judge advises on a release only for a rubric and language that reads ADVISORY on the lane. A new template hash, which every run name carries since 7.2, means a new judge that must be calibrated again. Labelled rows never tune anything. What changed on the lane: one Experiments run per calibrate call, and files under evals/reports. The footer names the next lesson.
  - **7.2 Separate offline checks, live scoring and LLM judgment (corrected M4 '7.1-7.3')**, Level 4, step 6 'Where the gate and the judge disagree', and the Level 5 box: Reads one new sentence, and the judge cell's new calibration line. *The learner sees:* 'not measured: run make judge-agree' beside each metric until 7.4, and a pointer saying 7.4 measures how often this judge agrees with people.
  - **7.3 Compare one controlled change against a baseline (corrected M4 '7.1-7.3')**, Level 4, step 5, the pairwise judge: Reads one sentence. *The learner sees:* The pairwise verdict's order bias is measured in 7.4 on frozen pairs, so a win rate is read with that figure beside it.
  - **10.4 Compare the LangChain and ADK adapters (corrected M5 'adapters LangChain vs ADK [10.4]')**, I3's level 'ADK's own tools: the dev UI and the evaluator', after make adk-eval: Runs make judge-agree TOOLUSE=1 ADK=<the adk eval results> AGENT_EVAL=<the saved agent-eval run of the same tasks> (Rs 0). *The learner sees:* For each case, the rubric judge's tool-use verdict beside agent_eval's computed trajectory-and-argument verdict, with agreement as k of n. The rule: where the path can be computed, the computed check decides and the rubric only explains. C4's handoff scoring already works this way, by trajectory matching rather than a model judge.
- **Proof:** python pagekit/check_lesson.py 7.4 and audit_pages.py 7.4 pass. The build asserts the people's kappa and the counts it computes from judge_labels.jsonl, and asserts each captured figure against data/lane.json. check_lesson passes for 7.2, 7.3 and 10.4. tools/build_workshop_demos.py --check, check_workshop_demos.py and M1's check_counts.py pass at the count that results (one more than at landing), Core hours included. On the author's lane, the rehearsal captures, with dates: per-rubric, per-language rates with intervals; the swap table; the pad table; and 10.4's tool-use agreement line on the same recorded invocations.
- **Risks:** The lesson count moves from decision 4's 66 to 67, which touches every stated count, and the new lesson's demo map must land in the same PR. The live step bills the Evaluation service for two requests per frozen row plus the swap and pad runs; the page states the cost from the bill, never from an estimate. Per-language halves are small (about 11 rows), so intervals are wide, and the page says so rather than hiding it. If the pinned SDK lacks the autorater settings, self-preference stays named, not measured. 10.4 is already long; its addition is one Rs 0 cell. This corrects the register's suggested home (a level in 7.2): 7.2 is already 8,992 words, and the corrected structure names 'judge vs people' as a slot of its own.
- **Decision:** A new lesson 7.4 for the 'judge vs people' slot (recommended), or a Level in 7.2 with the order swap as 7.3's Change-it. The title (proposed: 'Check the LLM judge against people'). The count moves by one from whatever stands at landing (66 under decision 4 if no other gap lesson lands first), Core by one and Core hours by 1.5, recorded against decision 4.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) I1's facts table records flipEnabled, samplingCount and whether the EvalTask path applies them, before 7.4 states them. (b) The manifest entry adds one lesson to whatever count stands at landing, and moves Core hours by 1.5 in the same PR. check_counts.py verifies the resulting count, not a fixed 67. (after review: The flip facts were unrecorded, and other gap clusters may add lessons first.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (a) 7.4 Level 4: by default the service flips the candidate and baseline for half of its samples (AutoraterConfig flipEnabled true, samplingCount 4). So the swap table measures the order bias left after that flipping, at no extra requests. If S2's second judge path exists, the swap run pins sampling_count. (b) 10.4's cell runs make judge-agree TOOLUSE=1 ADK=<the adk eval results>. agent_eval's verdict is computed on the invocations adk eval recorded, so both verdicts judge the same path. (after review: flip_enabled is on by default and costs no extra requests, and the tool-use comparison must use one trajectory.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The count of 67 assumed decision 4's base, and Core hours were never checked.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (The landing order sets the base count.)
- **Also (final review, consistency):** It lands before PR93, so that E11's final pass rehearses its 7.2 and 10.4 edits once (G-decision 4 (a)), and GP12 captures 7.3 and 7.4 itself.

  The page carries the hero chips, the table of contents, the 'In this lesson' box and L3's compact shared shell-setup section. 7.4's Level 1 opens on the Vertex AI Experiments run that make judge-calibrate writes, in the console, before the kit level.
- **Also (final review, CROSS-13: self-preference measured, not only named):**
  - Kit:
    - Q7's jury also runs over --frame rows. judge-calibrate gains JURY=1, which runs judge.py --frame <the frozen pointwise frame> --no-vertex --jury gemini-3.1-flash-lite,gemini-3.6-flash,gemini-3.1-pro-preview and writes evals/reports/jury_frame.json.
    - build_judge_set.py --capture keeps each answer's generator: the model the /v1/query response names, and CHAT_MODEL for /v1/chat rows. The frozen rows then read gemini-3.6-flash for the live arm and the chat rows, and gemini-3.1-flash-lite for the candidate arm.
    - judge_agree.py gains a seventh mode, self RUN. On natural rows only (constructed rows excluded), per juror and per generator, it prints:
      - the juror's lenience: its pass share minus the people's adjudicated pass share on the same rows;
      - its false-pass rate, where people failed at least 10 rows; below that it prints 'too few rows';
      - a Wilson interval on each figure;
      - the cross-over: each juror's lenience on its own model's answers minus its lenience on the other model's, flash-lite's beside flash's, with pro (which wrote no answer) as the reference.
    - --selftest recovers a planted own-model lenience on a canned jury.
  - Lesson: in 7.4 Level 4 'The judge against itself', the sentence 'Self-preference is named but not measured' becomes a cell after the pad step. It runs make judge-calibrate JURY=1, then make judge-agree self RUN=evals/reports/jury_frame.json. *The learner sees:*
    - each juror's lenience and false-pass rate on its own model's answers against the other model's;
    - the cross-over with its interval, and each juror's rupees from the run.
    - The page says the intervals are wide at this size. It also says what the result means: self-preference is each juror being more lenient on its own model's answers. If both jurors are more lenient on the same generator's answers, that reflects the answers, not self-preference.
  - Proof: judge_agree.py --selftest passes its self case in both CI workflows. On the author's lane, the self table is a dated capture that 7.4's build asserts against data/lane.json.
  - After: add Q7 (GP07, which lands in phase P5, before GP12).
- **Also (final review: slot definition, corrected M4 'error analysis and adversarial evals'):** 7.4's Level 2 opens with a new step, 'Read the failures first: name each one's cause' (Rs 0), before 'The people first'. The later steps are renumbered.

  The learner:
  1. runs make triage FRAME=evals/judge_labels.jsonl TEMPLATE=$HOME/triage74.csv;
  2. writes a note on the first ten failing rows before reading the class list;
  3. gives each row a class;
  4. runs make triage FRAME=evals/judge_labels.jsonl LABELS=$HOME/triage74.csv PEOPLE=1.

  *The learner sees:*
  - the frozen set's failing rows counted by class, each with its lever, the lesson that pulls it and what can catch it;
  - how many failures no gate rule can see (unsupported claims beside a passing figure), which is why the rest of the lesson checks the judge before it may advise;
  - their own classes' agreement with the two readers' adjudicated classes, as k of n and kappa, read as calibration and never as a score.

  A go-deeper sub-step, outside the 90 minutes, runs make triage REPORT=evals/reports/lesson72.json JUDGE=evals/reports/judge72.json ROWS=evals/reports/judge72_rows.jsonl, then REPORT=evals/reports/cand73.json, on the learner's own 7.2 and 7.3 runs. It compares the live revision's classes with the cheaper candidate's. For this, 7.2's judge cell gains --save-rows evals/reports/judge72_rows.jsonl in JUDGE_ARGS, in the same edit that adds its calibration line.

  Other page edits:
  - Level 0's analogy gains one line: before trusting a new examiner, the moderator sorts the failed scripts by the mistake made.
  - Level 1 gains four rows: error analysis, open coding, failure class, catcher.
  - Level 5 says that E3's failing rows in 10.4 and R5's labelling in 12.4 use the same tool and the same words.
  - 7.2's step-6 sentence becomes: 'Lesson 7.4 names each failure's cause, then measures how often this judge agrees with people.'

  Kit: build.py computes the class table from judge_labels.jsonl through triage.py. It asserts each count, lever and catcher, and the unsupported count the page prints. data/lane.json holds the author's dated triage of a 7.2 report and a 7.3 report, labelled 'yours differ'.

  Proof adds: the page's class table equals triage.py's on the committed file, and the template the page prints lists exactly the failing rows.

  Risk: about 15 minutes more of core time.

  Depends on S1's class fields and S2's triage.py.
- **Closes:** CROSS-13, CROSS-28

#### S4. Kit + 13.2: score the agents' live traffic with proxy signals and a sampled judge, and page on a cluster of failed turns, tripped end to end by a drill

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP13 · **After:** A3, K1, K3, K10, K8, G2, E9, M10, S2, S3
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/agent_health.py (new, stdlib) reads documind-chat's chat rows with the row reader E9's evals/router_health.py takes from evals/usage_rows.py:29-37. Per tenant and brain over HOURS it reports:
    - turns;
    - the share stopped by each limit (stopped_by);
    - the fallback share (K3's fallback_from);
    - the share carrying unsupported figures (A3's unsupported_figures);
    - the refusal share by kind (K1);
    - mean model calls and rupees a turn;
    - p50 and p95 latency.

    People and service accounts get separate columns. Each share is compared with the week before, which is the drift signal. The report says whether agent_alerts.tf's policy would page at its floor. --selftest runs on canned rows. Today the chat rows already carry stopped_by, refusals, tool_timeouts and cost, but nothing reads them.
  - agent_health.py --sample N --export DIR picks the N most recent agent turns from the lane's own sessions only: the project's service accounts and an OPERATOR email given on the command line, never another person's conversation. It exports each turn with K8's commands/thread_read.py: the question, the turn's tool results with their cited chunk ids, and the answer. S2's judge.py --frame reads the export, judge.enrich_context fills in the cited chunks' text from Firestore, and --save-rows keeps every rating. commands/agent-health.sh runs the three steps in their own environments (stdlib, ~/graph-venv, ~/judge-venv) and prints each sampled turn's groundedness beside the calibration verdict 7.4 left in evals/reports/judge_agree.json.
  - deploy/terraform/agent_alerts.tf (new) follows desk_alerts.tf's pattern and its rule that no label holds a person, a session or any text. Two log-based metrics are declared on every lane:
    - documind/chat_turns, on every documind-chat row with event chat. Labels: tenant, brain, stopped_by, fallback, and caller ('gserviceaccount' for a service account, empty for a person, by REGEXP_EXTRACT on the user field).
    - documind/chat_unsupported, on rows with unsupported_figures > 0.

    One PromQL google_monitoring_alert_policy, 'Agent turns stopped, fallen back or carrying unsupported figures', pages when that share over an hour exceeds 0.20 for a tenant with at least var.agent_alert_floor turns (default 100). Its count is var.agent_alerts ? 1 : 0, defaulting to false, and it notifies local.alert_channel_ids. Both thresholds are starting values, as desk_alerts.tf says of its own.
  - deploy/mk/operations.mk (created by G2) gains agent-health, with HOURS=, SAMPLE=, JUDGE=1 and OPERATOR=. AGENT_ALERTS ?= false and AGENT_ALERT_FLOOR ?= 100 are appended to TF_EXTRA_VARS, as mk/agents.mk:22-29 appends the Desk's switches. AGENT_ALERTS joins commands/infrastructure.py's SWITCHES (:35), so a plan that would drop the policy is refused. Update .PHONY and mk/README.md.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_agent_health.py (stdlib, in CI) checks that agent_health's filters and threshold equal agent_alerts.tf's, parsed from the file, as E9's test holds router_health to desk_alerts.tf. Canned rows must give the expected shares and the drift line, and --sample must never select a person other than OPERATOR. Run terraform -chdir=deploy/terraform validate.
  - lessons/13-operations/13.2-usage-reconcile/build.py prints agent_alerts.tf's policy beside alerts.tf's and desk_alerts.tf's lists. Its sentence 'chat is copied by the sink and read by nothing after it' is rebuilt from the readers the chat rows now have. Run python tools/kit_index.py.
  - deploy/README.md's switch inventory gains AGENT_ALERTS, AGENT_ALERT_FLOOR and AGENT_JUDGE_JOB, default off or absent, with the lesson that turns it on and the step that restores it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **13.2 Reconcile usage events, reports and alerts (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Level 1 (step 2): definitions: Reads four new rows: proxy signal, sampled judge, floor, drift. *The learner sees:* Each term next to the report or Terraform resource that holds it.
  - **13.2 Reconcile usage events, reports and alerts (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Level 4, step 6 'Alerts': a new sub-step after the dead-letter policy and PR80's router policies, 'The agents' turns, read at last': Runs make agent-health PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID HOURS=24 (Rs 0) over the turns the earlier lessons made. *The learner sees:* Per brain: turns; the share stopped by each limit, fallen back, carrying unsupported figures and refused; rupees a turn; p50 and p95. People and service accounts are shown apart, each against the week before.
  - **13.2 Reconcile usage events, reports and alerts (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Same sub-step: the drill: Runs make plan up with every switch the lane was applied with, plus AGENT_ALERTS=true AGENT_ALERT_FLOOR=5. Runs K10's make limits-drill STOP=model_calls, about eight turns with every agent brain stopped. Waits for the policy and reads its incident. Then runs make plan up AGENT_ALERTS=true to put the floor back to 100. *The learner sees:* The new metric's series, the policy's PromQL with its floor, and an incident opened by stopped turns. The chain from a turn row to a page is proved end to end, as the dead-letter drill proves its own. The router's policy, by contrast, counts people only, so its drill stays quiet by design.
  - **13.2 Reconcile usage events, reports and alerts (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Same sub-step: a sampled judge: Runs make agent-health SAMPLE=3 JUDGE=1 OPERATOR=<your email>. *The learner sees:* Three of their own recent agent turns, each with its groundedness rating and the calibration verdict 7.4 measured for that rubric and language. The page explains why the kit samples only its own and its eval accounts' turns: rows carry no content by design, and a person's conversation is judged only with their consent.
  - **13.2 Reconcile usage events, reports and alerts (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Level 5 (step 7), the checklist (step 8) and What changed on your lane: Reads why, what it costs and what the kit does not do yet, then checks two new rows. *The learner sees:* Why the kit uses proxy signals on every turn and a judge on samples, rather than content in every row. How a flagged turn becomes 13.1's reviewed eval row. That Monitoring bills alerting per condition and per series read, as the page already says. What changed on the lane: AGENT_ALERTS=true, kept on every later plan.
- **Proof:** agent_health.py --selftest, test_agent_health.py and router_health's tests pass in CI on the shared module, and terraform validate passes. On the author's lane, with AGENT_ALERTS=true and AGENT_ALERT_FLOOR=5, make limits-drill STOP=model_calls, run after the printed share and floor, opens an incident on the agent policy. That incident is the proof. It fails if the label extractor or the PromQL is wrong, for example if a null stopped_by does not extract as an empty label or an unsupported turn is counted twice. make agent-health HOURS=24 prints per-brain shares, and SAMPLE=3 JUDGE=1 prints three rated turns. check_lesson 13.2 and audit_pages 13.2 pass.
- **Risks:** Cloud Monitoring may refuse a PromQL condition on a metric with no data, as desk_alerts.tf warns, so the switch stays off until the lane has chat rows. A null stopped_by may not extract as an empty label; the drill is what proves it does. The drill caps documind-chat for a minute or two, and the floor change takes two applies. The sampled judge needs three environments and the DSN access that 11.2 already asks for. ADK turns cannot be sampled until H2 makes ADK sessions durable. 13.2 is also gaining desk_daily and the router's policies (PR80), so this sub-step must fit the lesson's time budget. This corrects the register's fix: an alert nobody trips is not taught, so the drill is added; the policy counts service accounts too, or the drill could not trip it; and the aspect asks for judges as well as proxy signals, so the sampled judge is added.
- **Decision:** AGENT_ALERTS defaults to off, is turned on in 13.2 and kept on (recommended, per 'new switches default off'), with a starting share of 0.20 and a floor of 100. The policy counts every caller (recommended, so the drill can trip it; the caller label lets an operator scope it to people later) or people only, as the router's does. The sampled judge reads only the lane's own and the eval accounts' turns (recommended).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) agent_health.py and E9's router_health.py import one shared module, evals/health_rows.py. It holds the row reader from usage_rows.py, week-on-week drift, floors parsed from a .tf file, and the people/service-account split. router_health switches to it in the same PR, as a small refactor if E9 has landed. Agent health is a second report over that module, not a parallel tool. (b) chat_turns carries 'unsupported' as a label (REGEXP_EXTRACT on unsupported_figures: nonzero or empty), so the policy's share counts each turn once from one metric; chat_unsupported is dropped. (c) Before waiting, the drill prints the hour's acme share and the floor, and states the precondition (few other acme turns in that hour). It is run a second time if the first run leaves the share under 0.20. (after review: Fixes: a parallel report broke the extend-don't-duplicate rule, two metrics double-counted a turn, and earlier acme turns could keep the drill under 0.20.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend 13.2's drill: the learner reads the printed share and floor before waiting, and the opened incident is the proof. If earlier acme turns keep the share under 0.20, the page says to run the drill a second time. (after review: The drill's precondition must be visible.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Comparing counts with gcloud logging read always held, because both read the same rows; the incident can fail.)
- **Also (final review, CROSS-14: the sampled judge runs continuously):**
  - Kit:
    - deploy/services/chat/agent_judge.py (new) runs on the chat image as chat-sa, as desk_overdue.py does. Each run:
      - takes the past hour's turns whose thread id names one of the project's service accounts as its user (the --sample rule; never a person's thread), at most AGENT_JUDGE_MAX of them (default 10);
      - reads each turn through the service's own checkpointer: LangGraph threads, and ADK sessions once H2 lands;
      - asks gemini-3.1-flash-lite on location global for an enum-only {grounded: yes|no} over the turn's tool results and answer, with Q7's juror prompt, through the google-genai already in the image;
      - logs one agent_judged row per turn: tenant, brain, trace_id, grounded, judge model and prompt sha. It never logs the question, a passage or the answer.
    - agent_alerts.tf declares documind-agent-judge, a Cloud Run job on var.chat_image with an hourly Cloud Scheduler trigger, in desk.tf's pattern (desk.tf:66-141). Its count is var.agent_judge_job && var.chat_image != "", default false. AGENT_JUDGE_JOB ?= false joins TF_EXTRA_VARS and commands/infrastructure.py's SWITCHES (:35).
    - agent_health.py reads the agent_judged rows. It prints the judged-grounded share per brain and per day beside the proxy shares, with its drift against the week before. Beside it is the verdict judge_agree.json holds for this juror's prompt sha, or NOT CALIBRATED. The paging alert stays on proxy signals, so an uncalibrated judge never pages anyone.
    - test_agent_health.py, on canned threads with a scripted juror: the job judges only service-account threads, logs no question, passage or answer text, and stops at AGENT_JUDGE_MAX.
  - Lesson: 13.2's sub-step, after 'a sampled judge':
    - Runs make plan up with every switch the lane was applied with, plus AGENT_JUDGE_JOB=true.
    - After the drill's turns, runs gcloud run jobs execute documind-agent-judge --wait, then make agent-health HOURS=24.
    - *The learner sees:* agent_judged rows that carry no text; the judged-grounded share per brain beside the proxy shares, with its drift; and the hourly schedule that keeps judging the lane's own turns with no command run.
    - 'What changed on your lane' gains AGENT_JUDGE_JOB=true, kept on every later plan or switched off.
  - Proof: the job's tests pass in CI, and terraform validate passes. On the author's lane, one execution logs agent_judged rows for the drill's service-account turns, and the next scheduled execution adds rows with no command run. make agent-health then prints their judged share.
- **Closes:** CROSS-14

#### S5. Kit + 13.1: turn the traced failure into a reviewed eval row, and test variations of it with simulated users before the next release

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P5, GP11 · **After:** E2, E3, G9 (including its agent-only stale-history branch decision; if G9 drops that branch, the step promotes G9's traced wrong-filter failure instead), K8, G2, S1, S8
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py (E2) gains --promote EXPORT --slice S --author NAME. It reads one session exported by K8's commands/thread_read.py --export, masks card and Aadhaar-shaped numbers with shared/pii.py, and drops the caller's email. It writes a candidate to evals/agent_tasks.candidates.jsonl with: source 'production'; the trace id the reader gives; the masked turns; a reference trajectory of retrieve on every turn (SYSTEM's rule, brains.py:51-58); an empty must_contain; and the reviewer as author. --check refuses a candidate with no must_contain or no reviewer. --accept ID moves a candidate that passes --check into agent_tasks.jsonl. Nothing enters the set unreviewed.
  - agent_eval.py --simulate (from E3's final review) reads persona cards from deploy/evals/sim_personas.jsonl (new) instead of one card held in code. A card has: id, slice, language, opening; facts given only when asked; facts never given; turns_max; scripted follow-up intents (ask whether the answer still holds after two turns; correct a fact mid-conversation); and a computed success check (a desk_calc value, must_contain on its corpus line, or a trajectory such as retrieve on the last turn). Six cards:
    - E3's acme E3 employee on Rs 52,000 with 7 years 6 months of service; desk_calc.gratuity_estimate gives Rs 2,10,000;
    - a follow-up card seeded from 13.1's stale-history failure;
    - a correction card (E3, then 'actually E2');
    - a card that never gives the wage, where success is that no figure is stated;
    - S1's two Hinglish cards.

    --vary N asks gemini-3.1-flash-lite on location global for N rewordings of the opening, in the card's language, through a response schema that returns an array of strings. Facts and success stay in the card, so a rewording cannot change what counts as right. The simulator's tokens and rupees print apart from the brain's, as E3 specifies. --check validates every card: must_contain on its corpus line, and desk_calc values recomputed.
  - deploy/mk/operations.mk gains promote (SESSION=, SLICE=, AUTHOR=), which runs make thread's export and then --promote. mk/agents.mk's agent-eval gains PERSONAS= and VARY=. Add the mk/README.md rows.
  - deploy/evals/tests/test_agent_eval.py gains cases: promote masks an Aadhaar-shaped number and drops the email; accept refuses a candidate without must_contain or author; the personas file validates; a canned simulator with --vary keeps every card's computed success unchanged.
  - lessons/13-operations/13.1-debug-wrong-answer/build.py runs --promote on a canned export at build time and asserts the masking. Then run python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **13.1 Debug a wrong answer through the complete pipeline (corrected M8 'observability: trace and debug a wrong answer [13.1]')**, A new Level 4 step 7, 'From one failure to a reviewed row', after G9's trace sub-step and before Level 5; the later steps are renumbered: Runs make promote SESSION=lesson131 SLICE=follow_up AUTHOR=<your name> and reads the candidate. Runs python evals/agent_eval.py --check and sees the candidate refused. Writes into it, as must_contain, the figure version 1 states. Runs --check, then --accept. Then runs make agent-eval TASKS=<the new id> BRAINS=langchain RUNS=3, after version 1 has been restored. *The learner sees:* The traced follow-up as a masked candidate with source 'production' and its trace id. It is refused until a person writes what counts as right, then accepted. The run shows how many of three runs search again on the follow-up: one anecdote turned into a rate the next release must beat.
  - **13.1 Debug a wrong answer through the complete pipeline (corrected M8 'observability: trace and debug a wrong answer [13.1]')**, Same step: 'Its variations, played by a simulated user': Runs make agent-eval SLICE=simulated PERSONAS=follow_up,correction BRAINS=langchain RUNS=3, and optionally adds VARY=2. *The learner sees:* Each simulated conversation turn by turn: what the persona said, what the agent called and what it answered. Whether the agent searched again on each follow-up and used the corrected fact. pass^3 per card. The simulator's rupees beside the brain's, all printed by the run.
  - **13.1 Debug a wrong answer through the complete pipeline (corrected M8 'observability: trace and debug a wrong answer [13.1]')**, Level 5 and the checklist: Reads the loop and checks two new rows. *The learner sees:* The loop: a trace, a confirmed failure, a reviewed row and its simulated variations, then the agent-eval gate the next release must pass. Why a row is never added unreviewed: a production conversation is a person's words, so it is masked and used only with consent. Why a 'self-improving' agent must sit inside the same gate. How S4's flagged turns in 13.2 feed this step.
  - **10.4 Compare the LangChain and ADK adapters (corrected M5 'adapters LangChain vs ADK [10.4]')**, E3's optional 'A simulated employee' cell in step 7: Reads one line instead of the cell, if the author takes the one-home option. *The learner sees:* A pointer to 13.1, where simulated users are taught on the failure they generalise.
- **Proof:** agent_eval.py --selftest and test_agent_eval.py pass, including the promote, accept, --ids, personas and vary cases. The promote case masks a Verhoeff-valid synthetic Aadhaar and a Luhn-valid test card with desk_rules.mask and drops the email. agent_eval.py --check validates sim_personas.jsonl. make -n promote-row resolves to the new recipe, and make -n promote still resolves to the documind-api traffic move. On the author's lane, the promoted lesson131 row passes --check and runs k of 3 on langchain through make agent-eval TASKS=<its id>, and the simulated cards print pass^3 per card with the simulator's rupees shown apart. check_lesson 13.1 and audit_pages 13.1 pass.
- **Risks:** 13.1 is also gaining G9's trace sub-step, so this step can push it past its time budget; the variations cell is the part that can become optional. Whether the agent searches again on the follow-up depends on the model, which is the point, and the page explains both outcomes. A promoted row carries words a person typed, and masking covers card and Aadhaar shapes, not every identifier, so the review is the real control. The simulator is a model and may drift from its card; keeping the facts in the card and the success checks in code bounds that. The Hinglish cards need S8's number words to be fair to the agent. This corrects the register's fix: showing the row 'failing on revision 2 and passing after the restore' would measure the document version, not the agent. The row is measured after the restore, on its trajectory and outcome, k of 3.
- **Decision:** Move E3's optional simulated cell from 10.4 to 13.1, so simulation has one home (recommended: it also frees time in 10.4), or keep both. Require a trace id on every promoted row (recommended, as provenance).
