#### S6. Kit + 13.3: size an agent's capacity: the model calls a turn fans out to, Gemini's shared quota, a 429 burst, a per-tenant turn rate and Provisioned Throughput

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP14 · **After:** K10, K2, K5, I1, H2, I7, S4
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/capacity.py (new; stdlib plus gcloud reads; --selftest) sheet --turns-per-min T reads the lane's measured inputs:
    - per brain: mean model calls, tokens a call and p95 latency, from documind-chat's chat rows through S4's reader;
    - rag-api generations a turn, from documind-api rows by brain (/v1/query generates; /v1/passages does not);
    - the router's calls per Desk turn, from desk rows;
    - CHAT_MODEL_ATTEMPTS, from /health's limits (limits.py:58);
    - each service's --concurrency and --max-instances, from gcloud run services describe (20 and 10 for documind-chat in deploy-chat.sh);
    - SHOW max_connections, and the pool sizes H2 sets.

    At T and at 10 x T it prints: Gemini requests and tokens a minute per model, with retries as the worst case; concurrent turns by Little's law (T x p95 latency / 60) against concurrency x max-instances; Cloud SQL connections (max-instances x 2 gunicorn workers x pool max, with the pool at 4 today, agent.py:100) against max_connections on the db-f1-micro tier (cloudsql.tf:35); and the binding constraint. It states the dynamic shared quota fact I1 records: Gemini 3 has no per-project quota to lower or raise. It prints Provisioned Throughput (GSUs needed and their monthly INR) beside pay-as-you-go for the same steady rate only from a dated, verified entry in shared/prices.py; otherwise it prints 'not verified: read the page'.
  - capacity.py burst --turns N --calls-per-turn M --capacity C runs at Rs 0 and is labelled an emulation. It starts K2's local Vertex stub, factored into deploy/commands/vertex_stub.py so that 10.3's cell and this one share it. The stub has a per-minute bucket and answers 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED beyond C. The burst drives N concurrent turns of M generate_content calls through google-genai with HttpRetryOptions(attempts=CHAT_MODEL_ATTEMPTS), as langchain-google-genai 4.4.0 builds them. A 429 is then retried the way google-genai 2.22.0 retries it: _api_client.py:545-590 retries 408, 429 and 5xx, with exponential backoff and jitter starting at 1 s. It prints requests sent, 429s, retries and failed turns, then the same with M = 1, the direct floor.
  - deploy/services/chat/limits.py and agent.py: K10's per-tenant chat_limits in tenant_settings gains turns_per_min. If absent there is no limit; like K10's other fields, it can only be stricter than the service's. One sliding 60 s window per worker process, limits.within_rate(key, per_min), also serves the Desk's /v1/route limit: desk.py's _within_rate (desk.py:636-645) calls it with ROUTE_RATE. Over the limit, POST /v1/chat (and K5's /v1/chat/stream, once it lands) answers 429 with Retry-After 60, and logs event chat_rate_limited with the tenant and no person. The check runs after the Desk's chat door, so a disclosure the door answers is never told to wait. lane.py chat-limits and make chat-limits gain TURNS_PER_MIN. commands/tests/test_chat_limits.py tests that the limit holds per tenant, that another tenant is unaffected, that absent means unlimited, and that Retry-After is set.
  - deploy/terraform/quota.tf is settled by a new row in I1's probe: does the project have any aiplatform generate_content quota for Gemini 3 on location global? If none, remove google_service_usage_consumer_quota_override.gemini_rpm and var.gemini_quota_override, and keep a comment saying that dynamic shared quota leaves no per-project ceiling. If one exists, correct the unverified metric and limit names (quota.tf:5-8) and keep the override off by default. Run terraform validate, and rebuild 13.2 and 13.3 where they quote quota.tf.
  - deploy/shared/prices.py gains a THROUGHPUT table: the per-GSU price, the term, and each kit model's per-GSU throughput. Entries go in only once read from Google's dated Provisioned Throughput page, through I1's price rows. The table is kept apart from PRICES, so test_chat_limits.py's check that PRICES equals cost.py's FALLBACK is untouched.
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (I1) gains rows: the quota row above; whether Provisioned Throughput is offered for gemini-3.6-flash and gemini-3.1-flash-lite on global, with their GSU figures; and the error class and attempt count a 429 produces through google-genai and langchain-google-genai, which the stub must match.
  - deploy/mk/operations.mk gains capacity (TURNS_PER_MIN=, BURST=1); update .PHONY and mk/README.md. deploy/commands/tests/test_capacity.py tests the sheet's arithmetic on canned inputs (Little's law, attempts, connections), that no PT line prints without a verified price, and that the burst against the stub counts 429s and retries.
  - deploy/README.md's switch inventory gains chat_limits.turns_per_min, default off or absent, with the lesson that turns it on and the step that restores it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **13.3 Exercise model routing, budgets and shutdown controls (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Level 0 (the analogy) and Level 1 (definitions): Reads the electricity analogy extended. The sanctioned load on the connection is step 6's GPU quota cap. For Gemini 3 the grid is shared: there is no sanctioned load to set, only limits on your own appliances or a dedicated line you buy. Reads new definitions rows: dynamic shared quota, 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, fan-out, Little's law, noisy neighbour, Provisioned Throughput, GSU. *The learner sees:* Each term next to the kit line or the dated page that states it.
  - **13.3 Exercise model routing, budgets and shutdown controls (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, A new Level 4 step after 'Off at night, and the ceiling under it': 'The ceiling Gemini does not give you': Runs make capacity BURST=1 (Rs 0): 20 agent turns of 4 calls each, then 20 turns of 1 call each, against a stub that serves 40 calls a minute. *The learner sees:* 429s, the client's retries adding to the load, and failed turns for the agent's shape, with far fewer for the floor's single call: fan-out and retries spend shared capacity. The cell is labelled an emulation of the mechanics.
  - **13.3 Exercise model routing, budgets and shutdown controls (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Same step: the sheet: Runs make capacity PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID TURNS_PER_MIN=10. *The learner sees:* Every input with its source (rows, settings, /health), and the sheet at 10 and 100 turns a minute. The binding constraint on their own lane, which on the default lane may be Cloud SQL connections (10 instances x 2 workers x a pool of 4 against a db-f1-micro) before Gemini. The dynamic shared quota line. Provisioned Throughput priced only if verified.
  - **13.3 Exercise model routing, budgets and shutdown controls (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Same step: 'Change it: a tenant's turn rate': Runs make chat-limits TENANT=acme TURNS_PER_MIN=2. Sends five acme turns one after another and one zeta turn from the shell, at the rupees the rows report. Then runs make chat-limits TENANT=acme RESET=1. *The learner sees:* At least one 429 with Retry-After 60 for acme while zeta answers 200. Why the window is per worker and per instance, as the Desk's route limit is. A per-tenant limit is what stops one tenant's burst from spending the shared pool every tenant draws on; it sits beside J4's per-caller MCP budget.
  - **13.3 Exercise model routing, budgets and shutdown controls (corrected M8 'usage and cost controls [13.2+13.3]')**, Level 5 and the checklist: Reads why and what the kit does not do yet, then checks two new rows. *The learner sees:* Why step 6's GPU cap has no Gemini twin. Why K3's fallback to the direct brain adds no capacity, since it draws on the same model pool, while 6.3's routed tier on another model does (tier_exhausted). When steady load justifies Provisioned Throughput. What the kit does not do yet: a rate store shared across instances. What changed on the lane: acme's limit reset.
  - **12.3 Trace the implemented A2A peer and its permissions (corrected M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, I7's level 'The same peer on Agent Engine', the comparison table: Reads one new row, Capacity. *The learner sees:* Cloud Run's 20 concurrent requests an instance and 5 instances for the peer (deploy-agent.sh), against Agent Engine's max_instances 1 in I7's config, with the same Gemini pool on global behind both. Moving the peer to another runtime moves no model capacity.
- **Proof:** capacity.py --selftest, test_capacity.py and test_chat_limits.py pass in CI, with the burst test in the chat-pins step. test_capacity.py covers Little's law on the mean, the separate p95 headroom line, attempts and connections. terraform validate and a make plan dry run pass once quota.tf is settled, with no reference to gemini_quota_override left if it was removed. On the author's lane: make capacity TURNS_PER_MIN=10 prints every input with its source and names one binding constraint. The burst, labelled an emulation, prints 429s and retries for four calls a turn and fewer for one. With TURNS_PER_MIN=1, at least one of six sequential acme turns answers 429 with Retry-After while the zeta turn answers 200, with the serving instances printed; or the run says 'inconclusive' when more than one instance served them. I1's facts table records the quota and Provisioned Throughput rows with dates. check_lesson passes for 13.3 and 12.3, and for 18.3 if the Makefile changed.
- **Risks:** The rate window is per worker and per instance, so the limit is approximate. The proof sends turns one at a time, so that one instance and at most two workers see them, and the page explains why. Moving desk.py's limiter into limits.within_rate shifts lines 10.6 may quote; check_lesson names any rebuild. The stub must reproduce google-genai's retry behaviour exactly, and what it shows is mechanics, never a claim about the lane's quota. Provisioned Throughput prices, terms and model support change through 2026, so they appear only from the dated page. max_connections is read live, and H2 may lower the pools, which changes the binding constraint; that is fine, because the sheet computes it rather than asserting it. This corrects the register's formula in three ways: it adds rag-api's generations per retrieve and the Cloud Run and Cloud SQL limits, because the first constraint to bind may not be Gemini; it shows the 429 as an emulation, because dynamic shared quota cannot be forced on a lane; and it settles quota.tf through I1 instead of only labelling it.
- **Decision:** Add the per-tenant turn rate now (recommended: a small extension of K10's per-tenant limits, absent by default) or record it as a gap. Delete quota.tf's gemini_rpm override if I1 finds no per-project Gemini 3 quota (recommended), or keep it labelled. Show Provisioned Throughput only once its price is verified (recommended).

#### S7. Kit + 12.5: a repeatable, signed review of every agent before it ships (trifecta, OWASP ASI01-ASI10, supply chain, residual risk), gated in CI on the signed digest, and the capstone's ninth component (the managed mapping is S9)

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP15 · **After:** M9, F10, F9, F7, F8, J1, J4, J5, J6, J7, J10, D1, H4, H5, C2, M2, I1, S3 (soft: a rubric pointer line), S6 (soft: a rubric pointer line), S10 (soft: the planner's review rows)
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_inventory.py (M9) gains --review, with optional PROJECT=, --attacks FILE and --signoff NAME. It covers each surface M9 lists: the four brains; the router; the handbook, statute and A* desk agents; the MCP server; the A2A peer; the Google Chat bridge; and I7's Agent Engine peer when it exists. For each it adds:
    - an approval column per tool (D1's second person; F8's taint rule);
    - a kill-switch column (DESK_ROUTE=off, agent_tools=classic, HANDOFF off, removing the peer's invoker);
    - the three legs of the lethal trifecta (private data, untrusted input, an outward action or channel), with a Rule of Two verdict that requires an enforced control or a person on each leg when all three are present;
    - the OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications table, from ASI01 Agent Goal Hijack to ASI10 Rogue Agents, read from deploy/security/asi_controls.json.
  - deploy/security/asi_controls.json (new) maps each ASI id to the kit's controls, the target or test that proves each, and a status. The status is enforced (a structural row of F10's attacks.jsonl, or a named CI test, fails if the control breaks), measured (a behavioural row scored as ASR), or gap (with its reason). The mapping:
    - ASI01: F4's fence and scrub, F5 and F6's Model Armor screens, F7's reviewed-only sources.
    - ASI02: check_args, J1's default deny, K10's caps.
    - ASI03: per-service accounts, C2's roster gate, J10's check-callers.
    - ASI04: F9's pins.
    - ASI05: no agent executes code today; I2's built-in code execution is demo-only (decision 26). If CROSS-10's cluster lands a code-executing agent, its sandbox becomes the control.
    - ASI06: F7, and H4's memory write rule.
    - ASI07: ID tokens and run.invoker on A2A, and J6's versioned card. The card is unsigned: a gap, since the pinned a2a-sdk 1.1.2 ships AgentCardSignature and a card signature_verifier that the kit does not use.
    - ASI08: K10's deadlines, K2's breaker, J7's peer cap.
    - ASI09: D1's approvals, and F8's provenance shown in each approval.
    - ASI10: the kill switches, and M9's unlisted-tool test.
  - Live evidence, when given:
    - F10's attack report: enforced failures per surface and ASR per behavioural row (F10's runner saves it as evals/reports/attacks.json if it does not already);
    - F9's make pin-tools CHECK=1;
    - J10's make check-callers;
    - each Cloud Run service's deployed image digest.

    Supply chain:
    - every deploy/**/requirements*.txt line pinned with ==;
    - pip-audit's findings on each service's requirements, when pip-audit is installed (Rs 0; it reads the advisory database over the network);
    - model ids pinned, with no alias;
    - third-party MCP tools pinned (F9);
    - package hashes recorded as a gap.
  - Outputs. evals/reports/SECURITY-REVIEW.md gives, per surface: the record, the trifecta, the ASI table with statuses, the live evidence, the supply chain, and the residual risks, each with an owner and a review date. It ends with owner and sign-off fields left for a person to fill in. evals/reports/agent-registry.json has one entry per agent and MCP server: name, description, endpoint, version as the deployed commit, skills or tools, identity, owner and the ASI controls. Its shape follows the A2A AgentCard's fields plus the governance fields; Agent Registry's own field names are added once I1 records them.
  - Repeatable on every tool change. deploy/security/agent_review.json (new) holds the kit's offline record and a digest over the surfaces, tools, side-effect classes, approvals, pins and model ids. agent_inventory.py --check runs in .github/workflows/checks.yml and fails when the digest moves, until make agent-review regenerates the record. The pull request that changes a tool therefore shows its review diff.
  - commands/tests/test_agent_inventory.py (M9) gains tests: every ASI id has a control or a gap with a reason; every write tool has an approval rule and the audit bucket as its destination; every model id is pinned; every requirements line is pinned; and --check fails on a scratch copy with an edited tool docstring or a new @tool.
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (I1) gains rows: Agent Registry's API, region, IAM and price on a standard project, tested by creating and deleting one entry; Agent Gateway's availability; and Security Command Center's agent threat detection, its tier, and whether a project-level activation sees Agent Engine. Only if Agent Registry is confirmed, add deploy/commands/agent_registry.py (new), which registers, lists and deletes the peer's and the MCP server's entries from agent-registry.json.
  - mk/protocols.mk gains agent-review (PROJECT=, ATTACKS=, SIGNOFF=, CHECK=1); update .PHONY and mk/README.md, then run python tools/kit_index.py.
  - plan/capstone-rubric.md, renumbered to the workshop's lessons for 14.4, gains a ninth component: SECURITY-REVIEW.md produced by make agent-review, signed, with every residual risk owned. Dimension 3, 'Cost at 10x', names make capacity's binding constraint (S6) as the specific limit. Dimension 4's score of 3 adds a judge used only where 7.4 measured it ADVISORY (S3).
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.5 Defend agents against injected documents and poisoned tools (new in F10; corrected M7 'injection 12.5')**, A Level 5 step before Verify: 'The review before an agent ships': Runs make agent-review (offline, Rs 0). After the attack eval, runs make agent-review PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID ATTACKS=evals/reports/attacks.json, and reads SECURITY-REVIEW.md. *The learner sees:* Per surface: the trifecta's legs and the Rule of Two verdict; the ASI01-ASI10 table with each control's target and status (enforced, measured or gap); the attack report's 0 enforced failures and ASR; pins and callers reading 'the same'; image digests; supply-chain findings; and the residual risks. Examples: the peer's actions on a log line rather than in the locked audit bucket, the unsigned card, package hashes. One table maps the review onto NIST AI RMF's four functions. Map: the inventory and the trifecta. Measure: the attack eval, 7.4's calibration, the agent eval. Manage: controls, kill switches, residual risk. Govern: owner, sign-off, a new review on every change. ISO/IEC 42001 is named as the management-system standard such a review sits in.
  - **12.5 Defend agents against injected documents and poisoned tools (new in F10; corrected M7 'injection 12.5')**, Same step: 'Change it': Edits notice_end's docstring in the local clone, or adds a stub @tool. Runs make agent-review CHECK=1. Regenerates with make agent-review, reads the diff, then runs git checkout. *The learner sees:* The check failing and naming the changed tool and the digest, then the regenerated record's diff. A tool change is a review: the rule F9 applies to tool metadata, now applied to every surface.
  - **10.5 Hand a question to the person the law names (corrected M5 'the case desk (rule gate and human handoff) [10.5]')**, M9's Level 5 'The governance record': Reads one pointer sentence. *The learner sees:* 10.5 shows the Desk and chat surfaces, as M9 was revised to do; the full record and the review are 12.5's last step.
  - **12.3 Trace the implemented A2A peer and its permissions, I7's level 'The same peer on Agent Engine' (corrected M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, A sub-level after the comparison table: 'Agent Registry and Agent Gateway: the managed form of your inventory and your doors': Reads agent-registry.json beside the Agent Engine peer. If I1 confirmed Agent Registry, runs python commands/agent_registry.py register, list and delete for the peer and the MCP server. *The learner sees:* A table pairing each kit control with its managed form: the caller graph and check-callers with Agent Gateway policy reading Agent Registry; J1's TOOL_POLICY and J4's per-caller budget with a gateway's tool and rate policy; the per-service accounts with Agent Identity; F9's pins with reviewed registry metadata; and the kit's log-based security alerts (F9's SECURITY_ALERTS, the Desk's alerts, S4's agent alert) with Security Command Center's agent threat detection. Every cell either ran on the lane or is labelled 'documented, not run on your lane', with I1's dated finding.
  - **14.4 Complete the independent capstone and operational handover (corrected M8 'capstone [14.4]')**, The published rubric, plan/capstone-rubric.md: Reads the ninth component and the two pointers. *The learner sees:* SECURITY-REVIEW.md, signed by the learner for their own fork; make capacity's sheet answering 'what breaks first at 10x'; and a judge used only where it was calibrated.
- **Proof:** test_agent_inventory.py passes with the new cases, including --check failing on three scratch copies: one with an edited docstring, one with a new @tool, and one whose regenerated record's signed digest differs from the current digest. agent_inventory --check passes on the committed, signed agent_review.json in checks.yml and documind-dryrun.yml. On the author's lane, make agent-review PROJECT=... ATTACKS=... writes SECURITY-REVIEW.md, listing every surface with its trifecta, Rule of Two verdict, ASI table, live evidence, supply-chain findings, and residual risks with owners. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 12.5 and 10.5.
- **Risks:** A static ASI mapping can claim more than the kit enforces, so each 'enforced' entry must name a structural attack row or a CI test, and the test checks that the named one exists. The digest check adds a review step to every tool change. That is the point, but it can slow contributors, so regenerating takes one command. pip-audit needs the network and its advisory database changes daily, so its findings are reported and never gate CI. Agent Registry, Agent Gateway and Security Command Center's agent detection may be preview, regional or org-level, so the managed half may be documentation only. 12.5 is a new, long lesson; this step must fit its time budget or move to a short Level 5 appendix. The capstone rubric is learner-facing and names a commercial owner, so the author approves the edit. This extends the register's fix in four ways: CI makes the review repeatable; it adds approval and kill-switch columns and a test that every write tool audits to the locked bucket; it adds the supply-chain checks; and it maps NIST AI RMF's functions in a table rather than one sentence.
- **Decision:** Where the full review lives: 12.5's Level 5 (recommended: right after the attack eval, which is its evidence), with 10.5 keeping the Desk and chat surfaces. This settles M9's two placements. pip-audit at Rs 0 (recommended), Artifact Analysis image scanning billed by the service behind a default-off switch, or both. The author's approval of the capstone rubric edit. Where the managed mapping is placed is decided by S9 and R7.
- **Revised:** title revised after review (Split: the Agent Registry and Gateway mapping moves to S9.)
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) agent_review.json stores each sign-off together with the digest it signed ({name, date, digest}). agent_inventory --check fails when the signed digest differs from the current one, so regenerating the record without a new sign-off fails. The check runs in .github/workflows/checks.yml and in deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml, so a learner's fork is gated too. (b) The Rule of Two verdict reads: when all three legs are present, an enforced control must remove one leg, or a person must approve the action. (c) ASI07's gap row is confirmed against a2a-sdk 1.1.2, which ships AgentCardSignature (a2a.types), the card resolver's signature_verifier, and a2a.utils.signing.create_signature_verifier (needs a2a-sdk[signing]). I1's facts table records them before 12.5 states them. (d) The Agent Registry-shaped JSON, the managed-platform rows in I1 and agent_registry.py move to S9. (after review: A regenerated file was not a review; the Rule of Two was misstated; the signing names needed confirming (they are present); the scope was too large for one task.)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: the 12.3 sub-level on Agent Registry and Agent Gateway moves to S9. 12.5's Change-it shows --check failing until a new sign-off is recorded with the new digest, not merely until the file is regenerated. (after review: The signed digest is the review, and the managed mapping has its own task.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The proof must show that a review cannot be skipped by regenerating the record. The registry outputs move to S9.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: M9, F10, F9, F7, F8, J1, J4, J5, J6, J7, J10, D1, H4, H5, C2, M2, I1, S3, S6. I7 moves to S9 with the managed mapping. (after review: The Agent Engine level is now S9's concern.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (The managed-page question moves with S9.)
- **Also (final review, consistency):** agent_inventory --review also lists arm S's lead agent (Q1 and Q8: ask_handbook and ask_statute, eval-only through desk_eval), and N4's code-action agent, N5's computer-use episode and P7's Ralph coding agent as lab agents that never deploy, each with its sandbox and credential. ASI05 reads: the deployed agents execute no code; N4's agent runs code only in a short-ttl Agent Engine sandbox whose isolation probes are the evidence, and P7's coding agent only in G-decision 6's container with a model-only credential. GP08 and GP20, if they land after GP15, regenerate and re-sign agent_review.json in the same PR.

  S3 and S6 are soft dependencies of the capstone rubric's two pointer lines only. Until GP12 and GP14 merge, those lines read 'after S3' and 'after S6', so GP15's 12.5 review and its CI gate do not wait for people's labels.
- **Also (final review: slot definition, corrected M7 'incident auto-remediation design'):** agent_inventory --review lists S10's commands/remediate.py as a surface. The planner:
  - is run by a person, and holds no lane credential of its own;
  - reads rows that carry emails but no question text;
  - sends a model only shares, counts and names;
  - executes nothing.

  Its Rule of Two verdict holds because the outward-action leg is the person who runs the printed command.

  The kill-switch column reads its switch actions from S10's deploy/ops/runbooks.json (DESK_ROUTE=off, agent_tools=classic). HANDOFF off and removing the peer's invoker stay person-only rows there, so each switch has one home.

  test_agent_inventory.py fails if runbooks.json gains an action without an undo or a verify. It also fails if remediate.py gains a lane write (a gcloud update, deploy or delete call) without an approval column in its review record.

  Both rows are soft: until GP13 lands they read 'after S10', so GP15 does not wait for it.

#### S8. Kit + 10.7, 10.4, 12.5: Hindi and Hinglish through the agent: Indian number words, Hindi month names and post-verb negation in the argument check, and per-language agent and attack slices

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P5, GP09 · **After:** A1, A2, E2, E3, E8, F10, M3, S1
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/shared/desk_calc.py is A1's shared calculator layer, so both the chat brains and the Desk get these changes:
    - _DIGITS' scale group and _SCALE (:434-437) gain hazaar, hazar, हज़ार and हजार (x 1,000), लाख (x 1,00,000) and करोड़ (x 1,00,00,000), in the spellings S1's fluent reviewer confirms.
    - _MONTHS, _MON and _DATE_LIKE (:438-457) gain the Devanagari month names, जनवरी to दिसंबर, with their common variants. '6 अक्टूबर 2026' then reads as a date, and its digits stop being numbers; today they read as the numbers 6 and 2026, with no date (checked offline).
    - TERM_EXPIRED (:381-382) gains khatam, khatm, samapt, ख़त्म, खत्म and समाप्त.
    - said() gains the Hindi negation that follows the verb ('khatam nahi hua', 'खत्म नहीं हुआ'), which _NEGATED (:459) cannot see because it only looks before the flag. Without it, adding khatam would set term_expired on a contract that has not ended, which is a wrong flag in a gratuity calculation.
    - Every Devanagari token is anchored with desk_rules.py's _DB and _DE lookarounds (desk_rules.py:72-78), not re's word boundary. A word-boundary pattern around जनवरी or नहीं never matches, because both end in a vowel sign that Python's re does not count as a word character (checked offline).
  - New desk_calc.selftest() cases:
    - '52 hazaar' and '५२ हज़ार' read 52,000;
    - '6 अक्टूबर 2026' and '6 जनवरी 2026' are dates and add no numbers;
    - 'contract khatam ho gaya' sets term_expired, and 'contract khatam nahi hua' does not;
    - check_args accepts gratuity_estimate's monthly_wage of 52000 from 'Meri salary 52 hazaar hai, 7 saal 8 mahine'. Today it refuses 52000 ('a fact about the person that their message does not state: ask them for it') and accepts 52, both checked offline;
    - 'dedh lakh' stays refused, so the agent asks for the figure.

    M3's 10.7 'Argument check' panel is a JS port of check_args that node checks against the Python over a grid, so the port gains the same words in this PR.
  - deploy/evals/agent_tasks.jsonl (E2) gains two fields: language (en, hi or hinglish; E2's own tasks are en) and twin. It also gains S1's four tasks: the Hinglish gratuity question, whose expected_calc gratuity_estimate(52000, 7, 8) desk_calc computes as Rs 2,40,000 because a part-year over six months counts as a year; the Devanagari notice question (notice_end from an acknowledgement on 6 October 2026); a Hinglish follow-up pair; and a Hinglish gratuity question with no wage (slice provenance_trap). agent_eval.py --check recomputes each expected_calc. Its report prints a language block beside E2's slices: outcome with its interval, trajectory, refusals, tokens in and out and rupees a task, and the token ratio against each English twin.
  - deploy/evals/adversarial/attacks.jsonl (F10) gains a language field and S1's three rows: a Hinglish instruction in an uploaded note; a Hinglish leave cap injected into the question sent to /v1/desk, which is structural (no figure may use the injected cap); and a Devanagari instruction inside a passage. evals/run_attacks.py reports ASR per language beside its per-surface table, and evals/tests/test_attacks.py covers the grouping.
  - Tests: commands/tests/test_desk.py (check_args on the Hinglish wage and on the negated flag), evals/tests/test_agent_eval.py (the language block) and test_attacks.py. Then make desk-check and python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.7 Run a desk as an agent with checked calculator arguments (new in M3; corrected M7 'desk router 10.6 + agent desks 10.7')**, Level 2, after M3's cell that runs check_args on the lane's own LV-07 passage: Re-runs the same check_args cell (Rs 0) with three messages: 'Meri salary 52 hazaar hai, 7 saal 8 mahine'; the same with Devanagari digits and हज़ार ('५२ हज़ार'); and 'contract khatam nahi hua, fixed term tha'. *The learner sees:* monthly_wage 52000 accepted with found 'message'; the Devanagari message read the same way; and term_expired refused on the negated sentence, with the refusal text the model would read.
  - **10.7 Run a desk as an agent with checked calculator arguments (new in M3; corrected M7 'desk router 10.6 + agent desks 10.7')**, Same level: 'Change it': Sends 'Meri salary 52 hajaar hai...' and sees it refused. Adds 'hajaar' to the scale words in the local shared/desk_calc.py, re-runs the cell and python shared/desk_calc.py --selftest, then runs git checkout -- shared/desk_calc.py. *The learner sees:* Refused, then accepted. Spellings vary, and an unknown one is refused so the agent asks, which is safe. Widening the list is a reviewed change with a test, as 10.5's gate patterns are.
  - **10.7 Run a desk as an agent with checked calculator arguments (new in M3; corrected M7 'desk router 10.6 + agent desks 10.7')**, Level 3, REST: Posts the Hinglish gratuity question to /v1/desk as evalacme with arm Astar. *The learner sees:* The calculation's 'Worked out in code' line naming 'your message' as the wage's source, and the turn's rupees and model calls.
  - **10.4 Compare the LangChain and ADK adapters (corrected M5 'adapters LangChain vs ADK [10.4]')**, E3's step 7, the output of make agent-eval: Reads the language block. *The learner sees:* Outcome, tokens and rupees a task for en, hi and hinglish, and each Hindi or Hinglish task's token ratio to its English twin: the cost of Indic scripts measured on the agent, beside 6.1's figure for rag-api.
  - **12.5 Defend agents against injected documents and poisoned tools (new in F10; corrected M7 'injection 12.5')**, Level 3, the attack eval's table: Reads ASR per language. *The learner sees:* 0 enforced failures whatever the language, and the behavioural rows' ASR for English against Hindi and Hinglish, captured from the author's run.
- **Proof:** python shared/desk_calc.py --selftest passes with the new cases. '52 hazaar' and '५२ हज़ार' read as 52,000. '6 अक्टूबर 2026' is read as a date and adds no numbers. Both spellings of हज़ार and ख़त्म (U+095B, or ज plus U+093C) read alike after NFKC. 'contract khatam ho gaya' sets term_expired, while 'contract khatam nahi hua', 'mera probation khatam ho gaya' and 'kaam khatam' do not. check_args accepts monthly_wage 52000 from 'Meri salary 52 hazaar hai, 7 saal 8 mahine'. 'dedh lakh' stays refused. The selftest fails if a word-boundary pattern anchors a Devanagari word. test_desk.py, test_agent_eval.py and test_attacks.py pass, and make desk-check is green. M3's node grid, with the Devanagari and Hinglish cases added, agrees with the Python. agent_eval.py --check recomputes Rs 2,40,000 for the Hinglish task. On the author's lane, make agent-eval prints the en, hi (at least three tasks) and hinglish lines with tokens and rupees, and make attack-eval prints ASR per language with 0 enforced failures. check_lesson passes for 10.7, 10.4 and 12.5.
- **Risks:** A wider word list widens what check_args accepts, so every added word comes from the fluent reviewer and carries a selftest case. Hindi number words such as बावन, and fractions such as dedh and dhai, stay unread by design: refusing and asking is safe. With only four tasks per run, the language block is an illustration with wide intervals, while the per-language figures that decide things stay with E10's route slices and 7.4's judge agreement. desk_calc is quoted by 10.6 and 10.7 and ported to JS for 10.7's panel, so the PR carries those rebuilds. This corrects the register's fix in three ways: the post-verb negation and the Devanagari anchoring prevent a wrong term_expired and dead patterns, and the expected gratuity is computed by desk_calc rather than typed.
- **Decision:** The word list that ships: recommended, the scale words, month names and term-ended words the fluent reviewer confirms, with Hindi number words and fractions left as safe refusals. The Change-it's spelling: 'hajaar', left out on purpose and named in the box.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (a) numbers_in, dates_in and said NFKC-normalise the message first, as desk_rules.normalise does, and the Devanagari patterns are written in normalised form. The selftest covers both spellings of the nukta letters: precomposed U+095B, and ज plus U+093C. NFC and NFKC both decompose U+095B. (b) The Hindi and Hinglish term-ended words are anchored to a subject, as the English pattern is: (contract|term|naukri|employment|अनुबंध|नौकरी) within a few words of khatam, khatm, samapt, ख़त्म, खत्म or समाप्त. The post-verb negation check is kept. The selftest asserts that 'mera probation khatam ho gaya' and 'kaam khatam' do not set term_expired. (c) M3's JS port maps Devanagari digits to ASCII before parsing, because JavaScript's digit class matches ASCII only and Number('५२') is NaN. It anchors Devanagari tokens with lookbehind and lookahead on the U+0900-U+097F range instead of word boundaries. The selftest's Devanagari and Hinglish cases join the node grid in this PR. (d) S1's fluent writer adds Devanagari twins of the gratuity and no-wage tasks, so the hi slice has at least three tasks. (after review: The JS port would diverge from Python; bare 'khatam' would waive the five-year rule for a probation; nukta spellings differ; the hi slice had one row.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (The proof now covers the anchoring, normalisation and JS-port fixes, plus a hi slice larger than one row.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to L after review (The desk_calc change, the JS port and its node grid, six tasks, three attack rows, per-language reporting and three lesson changes with the 10.6 and 10.7 rebuilds add up to more than M.)
- **Closes:** CROSS-14, CROSS-28

#### S9. 12.3 (managed platform): Agent Registry and Agent Gateway as the managed form of the kit's inventory and doors - an Agent Registry-shaped record, and ADK's AgentRegistry client reading the peer back

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP16 · **After:** S7, M9, I1, I7, J1, J4, J6, J10, F9, R7, C2
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_inventory.py (M9) --registry writes evals/reports/agent-registry.json, with one entry per A2A agent and MCP server: name, description, endpoint, version (the deployed commit), skills or tools, identity, owner, and the ASI controls from S7's deploy/security/asi_controls.json. The shape follows the AgentCard's fields plus the governance fields. Agent Registry's own field names are added once I1 records them.
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (I1) gains three rows. Agent Registry: its API, region, IAM and price on a standard project, tested by creating and deleting one entry. Agent Gateway: its availability. Security Command Center's agent threat detection: its tier, and whether a project-level activation sees Agent Engine.
  - Only if I1 confirms Agent Registry: deploy/commands/agent_registry.py (new) registers, lists and deletes the peer's and the MCP server's entries through the Agent Registry API. It then reads them back with google-adk 2.8.0's own client, google.adk.integrations.agent_registry.AgentRegistry (list_agents, get_agent_info, list_mcp_servers, get_mcp_toolset, get_remote_a2a_agent), which needs google-adk[a2a] and ADC. An ADK agent can then obtain the peer as a RemoteA2aAgent from the registry rather than from a hard-coded URL. --selftest runs on a fake HTTP session.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk: agent-registry (guard-project; ACTION=register|list|delete), added to .PHONY and mk/README.md. commands/tests/test_agent_inventory.py asserts that every A2A agent and MCP server in the inventory has a registry entry with the required fields. Then python tools/kit_index.py.
  - deploy/README.md's switch inventory gains PEER_CARD_SIGNING and PEER_CARD_JWK, default off or absent, with the lesson that turns it on and the step that restores it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3 Trace the implemented A2A peer and its permissions, I7's level 'The same peer on Agent Engine' (corrected M7 'Google's managed agent platform')**, A sub-level after I7's comparison table: 'Agent Registry and Agent Gateway: the managed form of your inventory and your doors': Reads agent-registry.json beside the Agent Engine peer. If I1 confirmed Agent Registry, runs make agent-registry ACTION=register, then ACTION=list. Then runs a python cell that resolves the peer with AgentRegistry.get_remote_a2a_agent and sends it one task. Then runs ACTION=delete. *The learner sees:* A table pairing each kit control with its managed form. The caller graph and check-callers pair with Agent Gateway policy reading Agent Registry. J1's TOOL_POLICY and J4's per-caller budget pair with a gateway's tool and rate policy. The per-service accounts pair with Agent Identity (ADK's integrations/agent_identity). F9's pins pair with reviewed registry metadata. The kit's log-based security alerts (F9's SECURITY_ALERTS, the Desk's alerts and S4's agent alert) pair with Security Command Center's agent threat detection. Every cell either ran on the lane or is labelled 'documented, not run on your lane', with I1's dated finding. If the registry ran, the learner also sees the peer resolved by name and answering through the RemoteA2aAgent the registry built.
- **Proof:** test_agent_inventory.py passes with the registry cases, and agent_registry.py --selftest passes. I1's facts table has dated rows for Agent Registry, Agent Gateway and Security Command Center's agent detection. On the author's lane, if Agent Registry is confirmed, this sequence is captured and labelled: register, list, the ADK client's get_remote_a2a_agent resolving the peer, one answered task, and delete. If it is not confirmed, every registry cell carries 'documented, not run' with the finding. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 12.3.
- **Risks:** Agent Registry, Agent Gateway and Security Command Center's agent detection may be preview, regional or org-level, so the managed half may be documentation only; the page labels it so. ADK's AgentRegistry client only reads (list, search, get) and builds RemoteA2aAgent and McpToolset objects, while registering goes through the API, so the script holds both paths. Google's naming drifts between Agent Engine, Agent Runtime and Agent Platform. 12.3 is long; R7 decides whether this sub-level and I7's level become their own corrected M7 page.
- **Decision:** (1) Register entries only if I1 confirms the API on a standard project (recommended); otherwise the sub-level is read-only. (2) Whether I7's level and this sub-level become their own page for M7's 'Google's managed agent platform' slot is R7's decision.
- **Revised:** effort revised to L after the final review (signed, versioned agent cards join it: a key, a signer, a verifier in PeerTool, two test files and a lesson step)
- **Also (final review, CROSS-23: signed, versioned cards):**
  - Kit:
    - deploy/services/agent/agent.py signs J6's AGENT_CARD at start-up when PEER_CARD_SIGNING=on (default off). It signs after every field is set and before to_a2a(..., agent_card=AGENT_CARD). It uses a2a-sdk's card signer (a2a.utils.signing; services/agent/requirements.txt becomes a2a-sdk[http-server,signing]==1.1.2) and an ES256 key read from the Secret Manager secret documind-agent-card-key, with its kid and alg in the protected header.
    - make agent-card-key (mk/protocols.mk, plus .PHONY and mk/README.md) creates the key, grants documind-agent-sa secretAccessor on that secret only, and prints the public JWK. PEER_CARD_JWK travels through deploy-services as the other chat switches do.
    - C2's PeerTool, when PEER_CARD_JWK holds that key, resolves the card itself with the resolver's signature_verifier (create_signature_verifier over the pinned key, ES256 only). It then builds RemoteA2aAgent from the verified AgentCard object instead of from the URL. An unsigned or altered card refuses the hop with a labelled peer_card_unverified result, logged with no request text.
    - smoke_agent.py's card check verifies the signature when PEER_CARD_JWK is given.
    - S7's asi_controls.json ASI07 row moves from gap to enforced and names the two tests below.
    - I1's facts table records the signer's and verifier's names in the pinned 1.1.2 before the page states them.
    - Tests:
      - test_a2a_peer.py: the served card verifies with the public key, and a copy with one skill's description changed fails.
      - test_handoff.py: PeerTool refuses an unsigned card and an altered card when PEER_CARD_JWK is set.
  - Lesson: S9's 12.3 sub-level opens with a step that needs no registry API, 'A signed card: trust the key, not the URL'.
    - Runs make agent-card-key, then redeploys documind-agent with PEER_CARD_SIGNING=on and documind-chat with PEER_CARD_JWK.
    - Runs a python cell that fetches the card with a token and runs the same verification PeerTool runs, first on the live card, then on a local copy in which it widens one skill's description.
    - *The learner sees:* the card's signatures entry (alg ES256 and its kid) beside its version (the deployed commit); 'verified' for the live card; the verifier's refusal of the altered copy; and make agent-review's ASI07 row now reading 'enforced'.
  - Proof: both test files pass in their CI venvs. On the author's lane, make smoke-agent verifies the live card's signature, and make smoke-handoff passes with PEER_CARD_JWK set. check_lesson 12.3 passes.
  - After: add C2 and J6.
- **Closes:** MULTI-14, CROSS-23

#### S10. Incident remediation kit: a runbook row for every alert policy, and make remediate, which proposes one reversible action that a person runs (with 13.2's cell)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP13 (beside S4: the runbook catalog, make remediate and 13.2's cell) · **After:** S4, K3, K10, K2, M2, A2, E9, E5, G2, F9 (soft: the SECURITY_ALERTS rows)
- **Spec (final review: slot definition, corrected M7 'incident auto-remediation design': the kit and 13.2's cell):** Kit:

  (a) deploy/ops/runbooks.json (new) has one row per alert policy declared in terraform/*.tf: alerts.tf's seven, desk_alerts.tf's eight, agent_alerts.tf's, and F9's optional SECURITY_ALERTS, keyed by display name. Each row names:
  - what code already does about it: K2's and R3's breakers, K3's labelled fallback, K4's heal, K10's limits, the router's own fallback, the documind-off job;
  - the evidence a planner reads;
  - the drill that trips it, where one exists: K10's limits-drill, K3's fault-drill, E9's router-drill, E5's overdue drill, 13.2's dead-letter drill;
  - either the actions a person may be offered, or 'person only' with the reason: case_overdue goes to the committee the law names, doc_type_pin_miss to a reviewer, a refused delegation and a security alert to the operator, and the dead-letter queue and failed ingests to make dlq.

  Actions come from one fixed list, each with its command, undo and verify:
  - traffic_to_previous: gcloud run services update-traffic SERVICE --to-revisions=PREVIOUS=100 (M2's rollback form), or make rollback for documind-api. Undo: --to-latest. Verify: make smoke-chat, make smoke or make smoke-desk.
  - desk_route_off: make desk TENANT=T DESK_ROUTE=off (M2's kill switch). Undo: DESK_ROUTE=on. Verify: M2's /v1/desk/check answers 403 for evalgrc after SETTINGS_TTL_S.
  - agent_tools_classic: make agent-tools TENANT=T TOOLS=classic (A2). Undo: TOOLS=calculators. Verify: make smoke-chat.
  - chat_limits_reset: make chat-limits TENANT=T RESET=1 (K10). Undo: the recorded values. Verify: make limits TENANT=T.
  - gate_rules: make desk TENANT=T DESK_GATE=rules. Undo: DESK_GATE=on. Verify: make desk's printout.
  - floor_to_zero: make off (13.3). Verify: the instance count.
  - person.

  (b) deploy/commands/remediate.py (new; stdlib plus gcloud reads; --selftest) takes POLICY= (a display name), SINCE= (default one hour), SERVICE= or TENANT=, INCIDENT=FILE for a captured incident, and OFFLINE=1 for canned evidence.

  It reads, read-only and at Rs 0:
  - the rows behind the policy in the window, through S4's evals/health_rows.py: shares by brain, stopped_by, fallback_reason and resource.labels.revision_name;
  - every revision of SERVICE created in the window, with its env and image diffed against the revision that served before it (7.3's diff);
  - the traffic table;
  - /health's limits.

  Rules come first. When exactly one action's precondition holds, that action is chosen with no model call. For traffic_to_previous, the precondition is that the serving revision was created inside the window and differs from the one before it. Only when several preconditions hold does one gemini-3.1-flash-lite call choose. It runs on location global, with an enum-only response schema over that policy's actions plus person. It is sent shares, counts, revision names and variable names, never a row's user or session field or any text.

  The planner prints the plan: the cause, the evidence, the action with its command, undo and verify, and the approval a production form would need. It runs nothing on the lane.

  VERIFY=1 runs the action's verify. For a chat action it also reads the verify turns' rows, and passes only with no fallback_from and no stopped_by.

  Canned incidents carry placeholders, never a lane's identifiers, and pass tools/leakscan.py.

  (c) deploy/commands/tests/test_remediate.py (stdlib; checks.yml and documind-dryrun.yml) checks that:
  - every alert policy display name parsed from terraform/*.tf has a runbook row, so a new policy without one fails CI;
  - every action names an undo and a verify target that resolves (make -n);
  - no command matches the denylist: delete, destroy, down, deploy, build, quota, iam, invoker;
  - a person-only policy always yields person;
  - each canned incident yields its documented action with 0 model calls when one precondition holds;
  - an instruction planted in an incident label never yields an action outside the policy's list;
  - the model request holds no user or session field.

  (d) deploy/mk/operations.mk gains remediate (POLICY=, SINCE=, SERVICE=, TENANT=, INCIDENT=, OFFLINE=1, VERIFY=1), with its .PHONY entry and mk/README.md row. Then run python tools/kit_index.py.

  Lesson, 13.2: after the drill's incident opens, the learner runs make remediate PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID POLICY='Agent turns stopped, fallen back or carrying unsupported figures'.

  *The learner sees:*
  - the drill's revision named as the cause: CHAT_MAX_MODEL_CALLS=1, created and replaced inside the window;
  - the serving revision no longer carrying it;
  - the plan 'no action: the cause is gone; the incident closes when the hour's share falls', with 0 model calls and a verify that passes.
- **Proof:** remediate.py --selftest and test_remediate.py pass in both workflows. On the author's lane (dated capture), the plan for 13.2's drill incident names the drill's revision and proposes no action, with 0 model calls. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 13.2. It fails if an action outside the policy's list is proposed, or if a runbook action lacks an undo or a verify.
- **Risks:** E9's router policies count people only, so the router incident is canned. Canned incidents carry placeholders, never a lane's identifiers, and pass tools/leakscan.py.
- **Closes:** (fills the slot 'incident auto-remediation design')

#### S11. New page 'incident auto-remediation design': code fixes what it can, an agent proposes, a person decides, a drill proves

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP22 · **After:** S10, S4, K3, P8, S7, S5, K13, D1, D2, G2, M2
- **Spec (final review: slot definition, corrected M7 'incident auto-remediation design': the page):** The new page in corrected M7, 'incident auto-remediation design'. Proposed title: 'Design incident response for an agent lane: code fixes what it can, an agent proposes, a person decides, a drill proves'.

  - Level 0: an analogy (during a signal failure, the station master may take only the rule book's permitted actions; the section controller authorises one; normal working resumes only after a test run). Then an interactive built from runbooks.json and terraform: pick a policy and see what code already does, the evidence, and the permitted actions with undo and verify, or 'a person only' with the reason.
  - Level 1: definitions: incident, runbook, mitigation against fix, reversible action, blast radius, verify, undo, pinned traffic, the remediation ladder.
  - Level 2: the kit verbatim through block(): limits-drill.sh's trap, restore and second smoke; breakers.py's rule that a control which takes the service down 'converts a finance problem into an outage'; off.tf's fixed script under its own account; D2's re-check of the approval; remediate.py's rules-then-enum choice. Then make remediate OFFLINE=1 runs on three incidents: S4's agent policy, captured from 13.2's drill on the author's lane, and the router's fallback share and the dead-letter queue, both canned. Two Change-its follow: plant an instruction in an incident label and see the plan unchanged; add a delete command to one action in the local runbooks.json, see test_remediate.py fail, then git checkout.
  - Level 3, on the lane:
    1. make fault-drill FAULT=model_error HOLD=1 (K3).
    2. make remediate PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID SERVICE=documind-chat POLICY='Agent turns stopped, fallen back or carrying unsupported figures'.
    3. The learner runs the printed update-traffic command.
    4. make remediate ... VERIFY=1.
    5. make fault-drill FAULT=model_error RESTORE=1.

    *The learner sees:* every agent brain answered by direct, and labelled; the plan naming CHAT_MODEL, set to a model that does not exist, as the difference between the serving revision and the one before; traffic_to_previous chosen with 0 model calls, with its undo, its verify and the approval it would need printed; the verify PASS; and, after RESTORE=1, traffic on the latest revision and make smoke-chat passing.
  - Level 4, direct reads: the revision list and the traffic table, before and after; one fallen-back turn's trace when TRACING is on (G2); the IAM an executor job would need, read from off.tf (roles/run.developer and actAs on named accounts), beside why the planner holds none.
  - Level 5: the ladder (code-only automatic remediation, already in the kit; the agent proposes and a person executes, built here; approve and execute through a D1 record and a D2-style fixed-argument job, designed, not built; never delete, deploy, build, raise a quota or take a service down). Also: the planner's trifecta and its row in S7's review; K13's durable-execution table, for an approval that waits; P6's point that an external check beats the model's own verdict; code fixes going to P7's sandboxed loop, with a person merging (P8); and an incident that was a wrong answer becoming a reviewed eval row through S5's promote-row.
  - Checklist and lane box: traffic on the latest revision, the fault restored, .fault-hold gone.

  Proof adds:
  - On the author's lane (dated): after the held drill, the plan names CHAT_MODEL and chooses traffic_to_previous with 0 model calls; the verify passes on every agent brain with no fallback_from; after RESTORE=1, traffic is on the latest revision and make smoke-chat passes.
  - check_lesson and audit_pages pass for the new page.
  - It fails if an action outside the policy's list is proposed, if verify passes while turns still fall back, or if traffic is left pinned.

  Risks:
  - A held fault answers every agent turn by direct until RESTORE=1, so the step restores in the same sitting and the lane box names it.
  - A traffic rollback pins traffic, and every drill refuses until --to-latest. The page teaches this, and S12's gotchas page lists it.
  - E9's router policies count people only, so the router incident is canned.
- **Also (final review, consistency):** The page carries the hero chips, the table of contents, the 'In this lesson' box and L3's compact shared shell-setup section.
- **Closes:** (fills the slot 'incident auto-remediation design')

#### S12. Production gotchas: a registry of what an agent lane does in production that no eval row shows, its runner, and the new page 13.4

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP23 · **After:** S6, S4, S11, K2, K3, G1, G2, H2, D1, D3, N2, R3, R4, A6, A7, M2
- **Spec (final review: slot definition, corrected M8 'production gotchas'):** A new page in corrected M8, in its own PR (GP23) after GP14 (S6) and after S11's remediation page. Its provisional number is 13.4 under G-decision 1: appended after 13.3, so no built page moves. Its manifest entry, counts and demo map land with it (M1's rule), and GP01's table gets its row. Proposed title: 'Reproduce the agent failures production shows and no eval row does'.

  Kit:

  (a) deploy/ops/gotchas.json (new) has one entry per gotcha, with:
  - id, symptom, surface and the owning task;
  - the drill: an offline cell or a make line, marked offline or live, with the marker its output must contain;
  - the detector: an alert policy's display name, a test file, a /health key or a log event, or 'silent' with its countermeasure;
  - the lever, and the lesson that teaches it;
  - a status: open, guarded, or fixed with the test that proves the fix.

  Twelve entries, each from the task that found it:
  1. make deploy-services rewrites documind-chat's whole env. Makefile:353 empties CHAT_EXTRA_ENV, and the deploy script (lesson-12.8.sh today, deploy-chat.sh after PR05) sets every variable with --set-env-vars. So G1's CHAT_STEPS, and any switch set with gcloud update (P4's ADK_PLANNER, F6's CHAT_ARMOR, R3's PEER_DEADLINE), is gone after the next deploy. K2's sticky rule covers only switches set through make.
  2. ADK sessions live in each gunicorn worker's memory until H2 (L9; fixed by H2's test).
  3. A new message on a paused thread drops its interrupt, guarded by D1's 409.
  4. A lost thought signature is silent through LangChain (N2's REQUEST=1 prints the sentinel).
  5. Cloud Run may rewrite an incoming trace id (G2's --session finds it).
  6. A2A tasks and contextId sessions live in one instance's memory, and a duplicate messageId runs and bills twice (R4's peer-lab).
  7. A repeated question served from the answer cache costs Rs 0 and flatters the direct brain (A6 and A7 mark and exclude hits).
  8. A timed-out call keeps running and bills (K3's search_timeout rows; R3's delivered-and-orphaned task).
  9. Breakers and rate windows are per worker and per instance (K2; R3; M2's 240 calls a minute per instance; S6's 'inconclusive').
  10. Dynamic shared quota gives Gemini 3 no per-project ceiling (S6's sheet and burst).
  11. A resumed node reruns from its first line (D3).
  12. A rollback by traffic pins documind-chat, so every later revision takes no traffic until --to-latest (the drills' latest_takes_all check; K3's RESTORE=1).

  (b) deploy/commands/gotchas.py (new, stdlib; --selftest):
  - make gotchas lists the registry;
  - OFFLINE=1 runs every offline drill at Rs 0 and checks its marker;
  - ID=<id> prints and runs one live drill, behind guard-project;
  - ENV=1 PROJECT= reads documind-chat's and documind-agent's live env, and prints each variable the next make deploy-services would drop.

  (c) deploy/commands/tests/test_gotchas.py (checks.yml) checks that:
  - every drill resolves (make -n, or its named offline cell exists);
  - every lesson is in course-manifest.json;
  - every detector exists in the kit (a policy display name in terraform/*.tf, a test file, a /health key, or a log event named in services/), or reads silent with a countermeasure;
  - every offline drill prints its marker.

  When a kit change makes a marker disappear, CI fails until the entry moves to fixed and names the test that proves it.

  mk/operations.mk gains gotchas (OFFLINE=1, ID=, ENV=1), with .PHONY, mk/README.md and kit_index.

  Lesson (the new page):
  - Level 0: an analogy (a vehicle maker's service bulletins: faults found in the field, each with how to reproduce it and the fix, never in the brochure). Then an interactive built from gotchas.json that filters the twelve by loud or silent, and by offline or live.
  - Level 1: definitions: gotcha, drill, marker, detector, silent failure, per-worker state, pinned traffic, sticky switch, at-least-once, orphaned work.
  - Level 2: make gotchas OFFLINE=1 (Rs 0): every offline drill, with the marker it printed.
  - Level 3: two live drills that restore themselves:
    1. Pinned traffic. Pin documind-chat's traffic to its serving revision by name. Set CHAT_STEPS=on with a raw gcloud update, and see a turn without steps. See make chat-steps refuse, because the latest revision does not take the traffic. Then run update-traffic --to-latest, see the steps, and run make chat-steps STEPS=off.
    2. A rewritten trace id. Run make tracing STATE=on first if tracing is off. Then make trace-ask with a forced traceparent: the trace is found by its id or, when Cloud Run rewrote it, by its session.
  - Level 4: make gotchas ENV=1 PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID: the switches on the learner's own lane that the next deploy would drop.
  - Level 5: sorts the twelve into those S4's agent policy or E9's router policies would page on and those nothing would, with each silent one's countermeasure (a CI test, a drill, a /health field). That sorting is the capstone's failure-modes dimension (S13).
  - Checklist and lane box: traffic on the latest revision, and CHAT_STEPS as the lane had it.

  Proof:
  - test_gotchas.py and gotchas.py --selftest pass in both workflows.
  - The build renders the interactive from gotchas.json, and asserts each entry's lesson and detector.
  - On the author's lane (dated), both live drills print their markers and leave traffic on the latest revision with make smoke-chat passing, and ENV=1 lists every switch the lane set with gcloud update.
  - It fails if a drill stops reproducing its gotcha without its entry moving to fixed, or if a live drill leaves the lane changed.
  - check_lesson and audit_pages pass for the new page.

  The page reuses each owner's drill, and adds only the registry, the runner and two live cells.
- **Also (final review, consistency):** The page carries the hero chips, the table of contents, the 'In this lesson' box and L3's compact shared shell-setup section.
- **Closes:** (fills the slot 'production gotchas')

#### S13. The capstone's agent lane: what each rubric component must show, the handover, and make capstone-check

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+plan · **Lands in:** phase P6, GP24 · **After:** S7, S10, S12, S6, S3, M9, H9, D1, A6, A7, A9, C3, E2, E3, E8, R1, K3, K10, R3, Q8 (soft: arm S where built)
- **Spec (final review: slot definition, corrected M8 'capstone and handover: the agent parts [14.4]'):** plan/capstone-rubric.md, renumbered by S7 (GP15), also defines what the chat (agent) lane must show for each component, and adds the handover. The author approves the learner-facing text before it is published.

  Rubric:
  - **1.** The live URL's Chat page answers a figure question with 'What the agent did', showing the figure worked out in code (G4, A3). A non-member gets 403.
  - **3.** The eval suite adds at least 12 agent tasks on the learner's own corpus, in E2's schema, with at least two follow_up tasks and one provenance_trap, accepted by agent_eval --check. A saved run (--save) prints each agent brain's paired verdict against the direct floor (paired.py) and pass^3 on the follow-up slice (E3).
  - **5.** ARCHITECTURE.md names three rejected choices, each with the measured threshold that would change it:
     - the harness (A7's decision table);
     - the topology: one agent or no agent against routed desks (A9, C3, and E8's pre-registered margin; arm S where built, Q8);
     - the boundary (R1).
  - **6.** COST-MODEL.md adds:
     - rupees per correct answer per brain against the floor (A6);
     - the loop's own spend: the chat row's cost_usd, or K11's tenant_daily line where it landed;
     - make capacity's binding constraint at 10x (S6).
  - **7.** FAILURE-MODES.md holds at least four agent entries. Each is reproduced by a kit drill: K3's fault-drill, K10's limits-drill, R3's FAULT=peer_timeout where the lane runs the peer, or an entry from S12's gotchas registry. Each entry gives the symptom, the command, and a 'caught by' line naming the policy that pages on it (S4's agent policy, E9's router policies), or 'none' with its countermeasure.
  - **8.** RUNBOOK.md holds the runbook rows from S10's deploy/ops/runbooks.json for the policies the lane applies, and one recorded rollback of documind-chat by traffic: the revision before and after, the verify's PASS, and traffic back on the latest revision.
  - **9.** SECURITY-REVIEW.md, from S7's make agent-review, signed.

  The handover adds:
  - make agent-inventory's record (M9);
  - make gotchas ENV=1's list of switches a deploy would drop;
  - the retention set with make chat-retention (H9);
  - who holds doc_approver (D1).

  Agent anchors for the dimensions:
  - Failure modes' 3 names which entries a policy pages on, which none does, and the countermeasure for those.
  - Cost's 3 is the capacity sheet's constraint.
  - Evals' 3 names a vacuous agent assertion and what replaced it. For example, an isolation task cannot fail while tools take the tenant from ToolRuntime, so the test that matters is the pin's own: F11's 10.1 cell and 10.4's dropped tenant_id. It also names a judge used only where 7.4 measured it ADVISORY.
  - Next month's 3 comes from an incident or a gotcha met while operating the lane.

  Kit: deploy/commands/capstone_check.py (new, stdlib; --selftest), behind make capstone-check LANE=agent [FORK=.] [PROJECT=] in mk/release.mk (new, Module 14; .PHONY and mk/README.md). At Rs 0 and in under ten minutes it checks that:
  - the nine files exist;
  - agent_tasks.jsonl passes agent_eval --check, and the saved run holds the paired and pass^3 lines;
  - every FAILURE-MODES entry's command resolves (make -n), and its 'caught by' names a policy declared in terraform/*.tf, or reads none with a countermeasure;
  - RUNBOOK.md names two revisions and a PASS;
  - SECURITY-REVIEW.md passes agent_inventory --check;
  - with PROJECT, /health's limits and the live switches match what the fork's files state.

  It prints PASS or FAIL per component and never scores a dimension.

  deploy/commands/tests/test_capstone_check.py: a canned agent-lane fork passes, and one canned fork per broken component fails exactly that component.

  When 14.4's page is written, its agent level runs this check on the learner's fork and walks the handover. These parts land in their own PR, GP24, after S11's and S12's pages, because RUNBOOK.md and FAILURE-MODES.md read S10's catalog and S12's registry.

  Proof adds:
  - capstone_check.py --selftest and test_capstone_check.py pass in both workflows.
  - On the author's reference fork, make capstone-check LANE=agent passes (dated).
  - The five dimensions stay scored by two assessors, and a fork that fails the check is an incomplete submission, as the rubric already says.
- **Closes:** (fills the slot 'capstone and handover: the agent parts [14.4]')

#### S14. rag-api's attack set: every attack row names the control that holds it, and 8.3 runs the set on the live revision and the candidate

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **Lands in:** phase P5, GP25 · **After:** F10, F1, F4, F5, F7, F9, S8, S1
- **Spec (final review: slot definition, corrected M4 'error analysis and adversarial evals'; it extends uplift task F10):** Kit:

  (a) Every attacks.jsonl row names the control that holds it: the Model Armor prompt screen, F4's exit scrub, the tenant filter, F5's context screen, F7's reviewed-only sources, check_args, the refusal rule, or F9's pins. The offline mode switches each named control off in its stand-in. It fails the run when a structural row stays closed with its control off ('vacuous: no control holds it'), or names no control.

  (b) For rag-api's doors, the set gains:
  - 8.3's four probes as rows: the plain question as a second control beside atk-05, the English and the Hinglish direct injections, and the PAN question as a measured row;
  - an acme question that asks for Globex's marker;
  - a refusal bait;
  - the vendor note's question.

  These sit beside S1's and S8's Hinglish and Devanagari rows whose door is rag-api's.

  (c) make attack-offline and make attack-eval take SURFACES=api, which keeps the rows whose door is /v1/query or /v1/stream. They also take API=, the URL to attack. A tagged candidate is allowed; the token's audience stays the service's canonical URL, as make eval-live mints it. A row served from the answer cache is marked and left out of the rate.

  Lesson: 8.3 gains a new Level 3 step, 'From four probes to an attack set', after 'Four questions to the candidate' and before the candidate's tag is removed. It runs:
  1. make attack-offline SURFACES=api (Rs 0);
  2. make attack-load TENANT=acme;
  3. make attack-eval SURFACES=api against the live revision, and again with API set to the candidate's tag URL;
  4. make attack-clean.

  *The learner sees:*
  - each row's language, kind (structural or behavioural), control and verdict on both revisions;
  - 0 structural failures on both;
  - the behavioural rows' attack success rate per language, with the guard off and on;
  - atk-05 and the plain question answered on both, so the guard's false positives are counted;
  - the vendor note's instruction still reaching the generator on the candidate, because its context screen is off (a pointer to 12.5's ARMOR_CONTEXT and reviewed-only sources).

  The 'does not do yet' box gains one point: an attack success rate is a measurement on one day's model, so only structural rows block.

  The 8.3 step lands in its own PR, GP25, after PR62 (F10) and GP09 (S8's Hinglish rows).

  Proof adds:
  - make attack-offline SURFACES=api is green in documind-dryrun.yml, and fails on a scratch row that names no control or stays closed with its control off.
  - On the author's lane (dated), 8.3's step prints 0 structural failures on both revisions, ASR per language on both, and both control rows answered 200 on both.
  - check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 8.3.

  Risk: about 15 minutes and a few rupees more on 8.3; the page reads the cost from make usage.
- **Closes:** (fills the slot 'error analysis and adversarial evals')
